## [LLMs Show Modular Cognitive Architecture Mirroring Human Brain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13567) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.13567) reports that large language models develop a modular cognitive architecture that parallels human brain functional specialization. Using circuit analyses across 46 tasks spanning language, formal reasoning, social reasoning, and physical reasoning, the authors found that tasks engaging the same human brain networks recruit overlapping neurons in LLMs, while tasks engaging different networks recruit distinct neurons. This finding suggests that modularity may be a fundamental property of intelligent systems, not just an evolutionary accident of biological brains. It bridges AI interpretability and neuroscience, potentially guiding the design of more interpretable and modular AI systems and offering insights into brain organization. The study employed circuit analyses across 46 tasks in four cognitive domains, comparing neuron recruitment patterns in LLMs with human brain network activations. The convergent emergence of modularity in both brains and neural networks suggests a fundamental principle, though the paper is a preprint and has not yet undergone peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are artificial neural networks trained on vast text data to predict and generate language. Mechanistic interpretability research aims to understand their internal computations, often by identifying 'circuits'—subnetworks responsible for specific behaviors. The human brain is known to have functional specialization, with distinct networks for language, reasoning, and social cognition. This study tests whether LLMs, despite being trained differently, exhibit similar modular organization.

## [Universal Thermodynamic Interatomic Potentials Enable Efficient Phase Stability Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14502) ⭐️ 9.0/10

The paper introduces thermodynamic interatomic potentials (TIPs), which extend universal interatomic potentials to Gibbs free energy models, enabling efficient prediction of phase transitions and thermodynamic properties in crystals. The implementation, TIP[UMA], is trained on free energies from quasi-harmonic to molecular dynamics fidelity and calibrated to higher-resolution calculations or experiment. This work makes free energy as accessible as potential energy, opening finite-temperature phase stability to high-throughput discovery. It could significantly accelerate computational materials discovery by enabling efficient phase stability predictions without costly ensemble averages. TIP[UMA] uses automatic differentiation to derive thermodynamic responses from temperature and pressure. It can return the equation of state of a crystal from a single evaluation and locate phase transitions among competing branches, including dynamically stabilized phases. Fine-tuning extends the model to alloy solubility limits and miscibility gaps.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Interatomic potentials are widely used in molecular dynamics simulations to predict material properties. Traditionally, free energy calculations require ensemble averages, which are computationally expensive. This paper introduces a novel approach that extends interatomic potentials to Gibbs free energy models, making free energy calculations more efficient.

## [AI Tightens Grothendieck Constant Bounds in Human-AI Math Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11195) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A research team used an AI research system to improve the best known bounds on the Grothendieck constant, tightening them to 6π/11 ≤ K_G ≤ π/(2 log(1+√2)) − 10⁻⁴. The AI produced insights that domain experts deemed novel, marking a significant milestone in AI-assisted mathematical research. This case study demonstrates that AI can contribute genuinely novel mathematical insights, not just verify or compute, potentially accelerating discovery in mathematics and other sciences. It also provides practical guidance for designing human-AI collaboration frameworks, which is crucial as AI agents become more prevalent in research. The Grothendieck constant K_G quantifies the gap between combinatorial optimization problems and their continuous relaxations, and its exact value remains unknown. The improved bounds were achieved through a long-horizon AI research system, and the paper details the strengths and weaknesses of AI in this context, along with conditions that fostered breakthrough insights.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: The Grothendieck inequality, introduced by Alexander Grothendieck in 1953, relates to the norm of bilinear forms and has applications in combinatorial optimization and computer science. The Grothendieck constant is the smallest constant satisfying this inequality, and determining its exact value is a long-standing open problem. AI agents are increasingly used in mathematics, but their role in generating novel insights is still being explored.

## [AI-Generated Copilot Autofix Introduces Command Injection in Snowflake's Jira](https://www.wiz.io/blog/red-agent-snowflake-copilot-cicd-bug) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Wiz Red Agent discovered and exploited a command injection vulnerability in Snowflake's GitHub Actions workflow, introduced by a GitHub Copilot Autofix commit on June 18, 2026. The AI-generated fix removed a sanitization pattern, allowing attackers to execute arbitrary commands via a crafted GitHub issue title. This incident highlights a critical shift in software security: AI coding tools can introduce vulnerabilities at scale, outpacing human review capacity. It underscores the urgent need for automated static analysis in CI/CD pipelines and raises questions about the reliability of AI-generated code in production environments. The vulnerability was in the jira_issue.yml workflow, where Copilot Autofix replaced a sanitized env var pattern with a direct echo of the issue title, enabling template injection. Wiz Red Agent exploited it to access sensitive data in Snowflake's internal Jira, demonstrating a fully autonomous attack chain.

**Background**: GitHub Copilot Autofix is an AI-powered feature that automatically generates code fixes for security vulnerabilities. GitHub Actions is a CI/CD platform that automates workflows, but its YAML syntax can be error-prone, especially when handling untrusted input. Static analysis tools like zizmor can detect such injection patterns before they are merged.

## [Anthropic's Claude Watermarking Sparks Debate on Writing Integrity](https://daringfireball.net/2026/08/anthropics_watermark_text_adulteration_in_claude_is_a_perversion_of_writing) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Anthropic announced that future Claude models will generate text containing a watermark to comply with the EU AI Act, using a technique that changes the randomness source during token selection rather than altering word probabilities. This move has drawn criticism from an opinion piece on Daring Fireball, which calls it a perversion of writing. This development is significant because it introduces a practical method for AI content provenance, but it also raises ethical concerns about the integrity of writing and privacy risks associated with watermark detection. The debate affects writers, educators, and AI users who rely on AI-generated text, as well as policymakers shaping AI regulations. The watermarking technique works by making the random number generator pseudo-random, which does not affect writing quality, as there is generally no 'best next token' with 100% certainty. However, checking text for watermarks requires sending the entire text to Anthropic, and potentially to multiple AI providers, raising privacy and data misuse concerns.

**Background**: AI text watermarking is a technique used to embed a statistical pattern in AI-generated text to determine its origin. Anthropic, along with other major AI providers, is implementing this to comply with the EU AI Act. The technique is invisible and statistical, inserted at the model level during generation, and is distinct from image-based watermarking.

<a id="item-11"></a>
## [New Metric Measures Cross-Task Behavioral Consistency in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13598) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced the Behavioral Consistency Metric (BCM), a novel metric that quantifies cross-task behavioral consistency in language model agents by analyzing feature-attribution vectors from execution traces. The study, based on roughly 9,000 trajectories from six agents on software engineering tasks, reveals that cross-task and within-task consistency are distinct axes that can diverge. This metric addresses a gap in agent evaluation, which traditionally relies on outcome metrics like success rate, by providing a process-level reliability signal. It could lead to more robust agent development and better understanding of agent behavior, with implications for AI safety and deployment. BCM trains a model to predict task success from behavioral features, derives per-trajectory feature-attribution vectors, and measures mean pairwise similarity within an agent system. The study found that consistency is not reducible to success rate, and that the frontier-versus-open-source consistency gap persists even when controlling for task difficulty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Language model agents are AI systems that perform tasks by interacting with environments, often evaluated by success rate. Behavioral consistency refers to how similarly an agent behaves across tasks, which is distinct from reproducibility within a single task. Feature attribution methods assign importance scores to input features, and here they are used to characterize agent behavior.

## [MobileMem: Benchmarking On-Device Long-Term Memory for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13606) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MobileMem introduces a benchmark and framework for evaluating on-device long-term memory in AI agents, based on a year-scale collection of mobile experiences. It uses a knowledge-grounded synthesis pipeline to create coherent, temporally consistent trajectories and tasks involving multi-hop reasoning, knowledge updating, and preference inference. This addresses a critical gap in realistic mobile settings, where existing benchmarks are inadequate for heterogeneous, multimodal, and evolving personal experiences. It could accelerate the development of persistent personal assistants that remember, understand, and adapt to users over time. ## [No Universal Signal Predicts Sample-Level LLM Regression Across Updates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13607) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper systematically evaluates multiple inference-time signals for predicting sample-level LLM regression under version updates, finding that no single signal is universally effective and that signal effectiveness varies by task and model update pair. This research addresses a critical gap in LLM deployment, as aggregate performance gains can mask individual sample regressions. The findings provide practical guidance for practitioners to choose appropriate regression signals for monitoring and selective fallback, potentially improving model reliability and user trust. The study compares single-model signals (confidence, logit margin, attention entropy) against cross-version signals (output KL divergence, likelihood drift, token-level KL, representation drift) using a unified added-value test. Experiments cover six benchmarks across three task families (MCQ, math reasoning, code generation) and six model update pairs, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: LLM regression refers to cases where a model update causes a previously correct response to become incorrect for a specific sample, even if overall performance improves. Predicting such regressions is challenging because aggregate metrics do not capture per-sample behavior. This paper explores signals available at inference time, such as confidence scores and KL divergence between old and new model outputs, to identify at-risk samples without needing labels.

## [Label-Free Evaluation of Agentic Learning Harnesses via Scaling Hypothesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13608) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a novel label-free evaluation framework for agentic continual learning harnesses, using the scaling hypothesis where a stronger teacher model provides sparse corrections to a smaller student, and scoring harnesses by the student's convergence toward the teacher. This addresses a critical gap in evaluating continual learning harnesses in cybersecurity, where labeled benchmarks are scarce and unreliable. The framework could significantly impact how such systems are assessed in operational settings, providing a practical proxy for true harness uplift when labels are absent. The framework is validated across security tasks, model families, and harness designs, showing that teacher-relative lift correlates with improvement relative to a held-out gold standard. It also demonstrates that LLM-as-a-judge between similarly powered models yields no usable signal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic continual learning harnesses pair an LLM with retrieval or memory to improve from feedback without retraining, which is valuable in cybersecurity. Traditional evaluation relies on labeled benchmarks, but these are often scarce, stale, and unrepresentative in operational settings. The scaling hypothesis suggests that model capability scales with compute, and this paper applies it to evaluation by using a stronger teacher to guide a smaller student.

## [Liability Rules Reshape AI Algorithm Design and Physician Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13618) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper on arXiv (2608.13618) formally models how a US liability rule for disparate AI algorithms affects an AI firm's design choices and a physician's decision to use AI in clinical care. The study finds that liability can lead to disparate AI use, with physicians reducing AI use overall and, over an intermediate liability range, relying less on AI for disadvantaged patients. This research addresses a critical intersection of AI, healthcare, and liability, with direct implications for policy debates on AI fairness and accountability. The findings suggest that liability rules, intended to protect disadvantaged groups, may inadvertently distort investment incentives and harm both patient groups, informing future regulatory design. The model considers an AI firm designing an algorithm for two patient groups, where improving accuracy for the disadvantaged group is more costly. The physician, as the accountable decision-maker, weighs reduced clinical uncertainty against expected liability exposure; the effect of liability on AI use is non-monotone, and mandating equal algorithmic accuracy can backfire.

## [Second Thought: Parallel Reasoning Boosts LLM Agent Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13667) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Second Thought is a training-free inference framework that runs four auxiliary reasoning branches in parallel with the main loop during the Action and Observation phases of ReAct-style LLM agents. It reduces average turn counts across all nine model-benchmark pairs and cuts main thread decoding by up to 43% in six settings. This framework addresses a key inefficiency in LLM agents by utilizing idle time during action and observation, potentially enabling faster and more cost-effective agentic systems. It could influence how future agent frameworks handle reasoning and execution, especially for long-horizon tasks. Second Thought forks four auxiliary branches after each Thought phase, decodes them concurrently with the main loop, and merges the generated thoughts when the environment observation arrives. Pass@1 shows no significant change in seven of nine pairs, with two significant improvements of +12.4 and +10.2 points; against a compute-matched control, it achieves higher Pass@1 with 1.3 to 3.2 less sequential decoding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: ReAct is a paradigm for LLM agents that interleaves reasoning (Thought), acting (Action), and observing (Observation) in a loop. In this paradigm, the agent's reasoning is typically frozen during Action and Observation phases, creating an idle window that Second Thought exploits. This work is part of a broader trend in optimizing LLM inference and agent efficiency, such as parallel decoding and concurrent attention mechanisms.

A pre-registered study on GPT-2 small shows that circuit discovery results are highly unstable across defensible analytic choices, with 73.2% of specification pairs yielding different claims. This undermines the reliability of mechanistic interpretability as evidence for EU AI Act compliance, potentially affecting regulatory practices and AI safety efforts. The study used 15,840 pre-registered specifications, and even standardizing the evaluation metric left a flip rate of 59.4%. The circuits were structurally near-disjoint (median Jaccard overlap 4%) and functionally uncorrelated (Cohen's kappa 0.015).

## [SocialRL: Training Small Models to Negotiate at Frontier Level](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13787) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SocialRL, a new training recipe, trains a 4B-parameter language model to negotiate strategically across six domains, matching or exceeding GPT-4.1, GPT-5.1, and GPT-5.2 on held-out scenarios. The unified model achieves 0.627 average utility, surpassing GPT-4.1 (0.625), GPT-5.1 (0.619), and GPT-5.2 (0.613). This work addresses a critical gap in AI agent behavior: friendly assistants often make poor delegates in negotiation tasks, disclosing private information or conceding too easily. By enabling small models to achieve frontier-level social reasoning, SocialRL could make AI agents more effective in real-world principal-driven tasks like scheduling, bargaining, and recruiting. The training recipe includes cascade RL and multi-teacher on-policy distillation (OPD) to consolidate per-domain specialists into a single unified model. An explicit theory-of-mind (ToM) scaffold helps only during training; distilling the ToM trace, rather than actions alone, improves utility and generalization across environments.

These tasks often place the agent opposite a counterpart with conflicting goals, requiring strategic social reasoning. SocialRL trains social reasoning directly, using reinforcement learning and distillation, to improve negotiation performance in small language models.

## [TANGLE Benchmark Tests LLM Agents on Irreducible Memory Conflicts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13921) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces TANGLE, a benchmark with 541 instances across 40 personas and three conflict types, to evaluate whether LLM agents recognize and appropriately handle genuinely unresolvable memory conflicts. It also proposes a Conflict-Aware Action Policy (CAAP) that adapts actions to each conflict using available evidence. This work addresses a significant underexplored problem in AI: when personal memory conflicts cannot be resolved, forcing a single answer leads to overconfident and unjustified actions. The benchmark and policy could guide the development of more robust and honest AI agents that acknowledge uncertainty and seek clarification when needed. TANGLE evaluates agents on five dimensions: conflict perception, causal reasoning, confidence calibration, clarification seeking, and memory faithfulness. Experiments show that with curated memory, models recognize conflicts better than they calibrate actions or seek targeted clarification, while pipeline memory extraction fails to preserve conflict-bearing relations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents increasingly maintain personal memory across sessions, but preferences can change, behavior can evolve, and sources can conflict. When a query lacks context, time, or source authority, treating one memory as definitive converts unresolved conflict into an unjustified, overconfident action. Existing benchmarks typically recover a single answer from conflicting evidence, overlooking whether agents recognize underdetermination and preserve alternatives.

## [Structural Abstention: Preventing Fabricated Values in LLM Database Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13926) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes a novel architectural pattern called 'trusted kernel with a generative shell' for LLM-based text-to-SQL systems, ensuring that a component that can fabricate may influence which question is answered but never which value is returned. This pattern, termed 'structural abstention', is specified implementation-independently and validated with a two-year production case study. This addresses a critical reliability issue in enterprise AI deployments where LLM text-to-SQL hallucinations can produce fluent but wrong answers that are indistinguishable from correct ones. By ensuring that unanswerable requests are declined rather than approximated, this pattern could significantly enhance trust and safety in AI-driven data access systems. The pattern rests on the invariant that a generative shell interprets underspecified input and phrases replies, while a deterministic kernel matches fully specified questions against a bounded set of answerable question shapes and compiles them to queries via deterministic execution. The paper provides a five-decision recipe, extends the invariant to agentic systems, and reports a two-year production case study alongside two generative alternatives.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Natural Language Interfaces to Databases (NLIDB) allow users to query databases using natural language, often converting text to SQL. However, LLM-based text-to-SQL systems are prone to hallucinations, generating executable but semantically unfaithful SQL, which can lead to incorrect answers that are hard to detect. This reliability problem is especially critical in enterprise settings where users cannot inspect the generated queries.

## [AI Research Preference Models Optimize Compute Allocation in Autonomous ML Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13940) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces AI Research Preference Models (RPMs), which predict which candidate solutions are most worth executing in AI research agents, without executing them all. Two variants—an inference-only model and an agentic model that runs small pilot experiments—are integrated into the AIRA-dojo search agent, raising average normalized scores from 0.684 to 0.711 and 0.729 respectively on AIRS-Bench. This addresses a critical bottleneck in automated ML experimentation: the high cost of evaluating candidate solutions. By predicting value before execution, RPMs can significantly reduce compute usage and speed up research, potentially accelerating progress in AI-driven scientific discovery and making autonomous research agents more practical. The RPMs are built from frozen pretrained language models with no task-specific training. The agentic variant runs small-scale pilot experiments before deciding, and the best RPMs achieve new state-of-the-art results on two AIRS-Bench tasks, reaching the unguided agent's 24-hour performance in about 15 hours using less than two-thirds of its execution budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

The paper leverages frozen pretrained language models to avoid task-specific training, and the agentic variant incorporates pilot experiments for better decisions.

## [News Is Priced In Before Publication, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14014) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study analyzing 4.57 million financial news articles (2023-2026) for about 3,000 US stocks finds that price moves are largely priced in before publication, with the cumulative move by publication day being 2.8 times its value 20 days later. The study uses a large language model teacher distilled into a compact classifier via active learning to tag events. This research provides large-scale empirical evidence on market efficiency, showing that markets absorb public information quickly, often before publication. It also reveals differential drift patterns: markets underreact to quantified fundamental news but overreact to soft story-driven news, which has practical implications for trading strategies and forecasting models. The study clusters articles into stories to separate first reports from follow-ups, and measures beta-adjusted abnormal returns around 1.68 million stock-day events, with 364,405 neutral-sentiment events as a placebo. It also finds that publicity raises volatility before publication day and declines afterward, and provides a table of measured drift for each event tag usable as a prior in forecasting models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: The 'buy the rumor, sell the news' adage suggests that price moves occur before and at news publication, not after. Event study methodology in finance measures the impact of an event by examining abnormal returns around the event date. Beta-adjusted abnormal returns account for market movements and systematic risk, isolating the effect of the news. Active learning in NLP is a technique to efficiently train classifiers by selecting the most informative data points for labeling.

## [When and Why LLM Agent Skills Help—and Fail](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14036) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper systematically investigates when and why skills help LLM agents, using controlled experiments and trajectory analysis. It introduces a taxonomy of twelve skill-use modes and finds that skills primarily work as procedural anchors, stabilizing execution rather than injecting missing facts. This research moves evaluation beyond aggregate success rates, offering a nuanced understanding of skill effectiveness that can guide the development of more reliable self-evolving agents. It also highlights retrieval as a separate bottleneck, which is crucial for designing robust agent systems. The study normalizes 8,135 trial records and retains 238 valid unique labels from 240 open-coded records, consolidating them into a taxonomy of three high-level categories and twelve skill-use modes. Skills improve over Workflow Memory by 6.06 points in matched comparisons, and procedural anchoring accounts for 65.7% of skill cases, while retrieval precision falls from 29.6% to 3.3% as pools grow from 5 to 100.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Skills are structured packages of knowledge that enhance LLM agents at inference time. Previous evaluations mostly measured whether skills improve aggregated task success, leaving questions about when and why they work unanswered. This paper addresses that gap by isolating the effects of representation, outcome annotation, retrieval difficulty, and cross-framework robustness.

## [Optimal Domain Data Repetition Rises with Model Size](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14071) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.14071) empirically shows that in LLM pretraining, the optimal repetition count for high-quality domain data increases mildly with model size at a fixed tokens-per-parameter (TPP) ratio, and is strongly negatively correlated with the domain's final validation loss. This finding provides practical guidance for scaling LLM pretraining when high-quality domain data is limited, helping researchers and engineers decide how many times to repeat such data to avoid overfitting while maintaining performance. It also suggests that repetition counts tuned on smaller proxy models can be transferred to larger models, saving computational resources. The study finds that the optimal repetition count is only weakly related to the amount of unique domain data, but strongly negatively correlated with the domain's final validation loss. These insights are based on experiments where the training-token budget grows proportionally with model size, maintaining a fixed TPP ratio.

## [Mandato: Protocol-Level Enforcement of Signed AI Agent Mandates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14074) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Mandato introduces a governance proxy that enforces digitally signed mandates on AI agent actions at the protocol level, using a hash-chained audit log for tamper-evident records. The system is designed to be transparent to the Model Context Protocol (MCP) and maps to EU AI Act, GDPR, NIS2, and eIDAS 2. This addresses a critical gap in AI agent governance by moving authorization from application code to a verifiable, protocol-level layer, enhancing accountability and auditability. It is particularly relevant as MCP and agentic AI become widespread, offering a path to regulatory compliance and evidentiary-grade logging. The mandate is a machine-readable, cryptographically signed artifact specifying tool permissions, parameter constraints, and contextual conditions, evaluated against a mandate chain. The audit log is append-only and hash-chained, with periodic anchoring via qualified timestamps, and the architecture separates decision and enforcement points.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents often use protocols like MCP to interact with external tools, but authorization logic typically resides in application code, lacking cryptographic signing and independent auditability. Hash-chained audit logs provide tamper-evidence by linking each entry to the previous one, making alterations detectable. Governance proxies act as intermediaries to enforce policies and log actions.

<a id="item-27"></a>
## [Regime-Conditional Verification Adapts Safety Classifiers Without Retraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14089) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Regime-Conditional Verification (RCV), a lightweight wrapper that adapts off-the-shelf safety classifiers to a deployer's policy without retraining. RCV estimates the probability of disagreement with the deployer's policy from internal representations and selectively corrects predictions, also providing a label-free signal for detecting distribution shift. This method addresses a critical issue in LLM deployment: safety classifiers often fail due to policy mismatch and performance degradation over time. By enabling adaptation without retraining, RCV offers a practical, cost-effective solution that improves safety adherence and detects distribution shift, potentially benefiting a wide range of AI systems. Across three off-the-shelf safety classifiers and two benchmark datasets, RCV improved adherence in every classifier-dataset combination, catching up to 0.81 of previously missed unsafe content. In a deployment study with ten attack campaigns, RCV detected every campaign in a dedicated injection panel, and most drift episodes were repaired without updating the classifier, reserving fine-tuning for residual episodes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Safety classifiers are used to filter harmful content from LLM outputs, but they often reflect the training policy rather than the deployer's desired policy, and their performance degrades as deployment traffic evolves. Distribution shift refers to changes in the input data distribution that can cause model performance to drop. RCV leverages internal representations to estimate correctness and detect shift, enabling a maintenance loop that updates the estimation layer and resorts to fine-tuning only when necessary.

<a id="item-28"></a>
## [FreeBalance: Pre-Routing MoE Load Balancing via Residual Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14205) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FreeBalance introduces a lossless online load-balancing framework for Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models that predicts routing distributions before routing decisions are made, enabling expert migration to overlap with preceding computation stages. Experiments show it reduces the max-to-mean rank load ratio by 32.8% and end-to-end prefill latency by 13.1%. This addresses a critical performance bottleneck in MoE inference, where load imbalance stalls global execution due to skewed routing. By overlapping migration with computation, FreeBalance can significantly reduce latency, benefiting distributed inference systems for large language models. FreeBalance leverages cross-layer similarities in hidden representations within the residual network to build a lightweight workload predictor. A cost model constrains the number of swaps to fully hide synchronization overhead within the available window, and the method hides balancing overhead for an average of 5.1 experts per layer, which would otherwise account for about 8.5% of critical-path latency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: In expert-parallel MoE serving, load imbalance arises because routing distributions are often skewed, causing some experts to receive far more tokens than others, leading to stragglers and increased latency. Traditional online load balancing methods rely on routing statistics collected after each MoE router, placing migration overhead on the critical path. FreeBalance aims to predict routing distributions in advance to overlap migration with computation, avoiding this overhead.

## [MathForm: Scaling Autoformalization with Retrieval and Verification-Guided Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14221) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MathForm introduces a framework that combines Mathlib knowledge retrieval with verification-guided iterative refinement to generate verified training data for autoformalization. The authors constructed FormalVerse, a Lean 4 dataset of approximately 367K verified examples, and trained MathForm-8B, which outperforms specialized 32B autoformalizers on six benchmarks. This work addresses a key bottleneck in autoformalization by reducing reliance on parametric memory and enabling feedback-driven revision, which could improve the reliability and scalability of formal verification workflows. It also demonstrates that smaller models can achieve state-of-the-art results with better data and training strategies, potentially lowering computational barriers for the field. MathForm-8B achieves average Pass@8 rates of 88.06% under Syntax Check (SC) and 72.37% under Consistency Check (CC) across six benchmarks. On the challenging FATE-H and FATE-X subsets, it attains CC pass rates of 63% and 37%, exceeding the strongest specialized baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Autoformalization is the task of translating natural-language mathematical statements into machine-verifiable formal languages like Lean 4. Existing approaches often rely on the model's parametric memory for library-specific knowledge and use single-pass filtering, lacking feedback-driven revision. MathForm addresses these issues by using a retrieval planner to gather relevant definitions from Mathlib and by refining generated statements using compiler diagnostics and semantic-consistency feedback.

## [TimeSage-EV: Live Benchmark for Agentic Time Series Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14270) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TimeSage-EV is a new live benchmark for evaluating LLM agents on time series analysis in evolving environments, tracking 60 real institutional scenarios across 6 domains with 1,485 scenario-period QA pairs from Feb 2023 to May 2026. It also introduces TimeSage-1.0, a self-evolving agent with a reusable analytical skill library, and reveals significant performance gaps across model tiers. This benchmark addresses the underexplored issue of temporal validity in time series QA, where new data releases can change conclusions. It provides a substantial dataset and evaluates frontier LLM agents, which could influence future benchmark design and improve the reliability of AI-driven time series analysis in high-stakes domains. The benchmark spans monthly, weekly, daily, and irregular release cadences, and evaluates state identification, data summarization, and outlook reasoning. Experiments with frontier LLM agents and TimeSage-1.0 show recurring failures in temporal validity, exogenous context use, and adaptation, and the resource includes monthly updates, code, a leaderboard, and failure-mode analyses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Time series analysis in high-stakes domains relies on recurring data releases, where new observations can alter the evidence base. Existing time series QA benchmarks typically use fixed snapshots, failing to evaluate temporal validity and cutoff-aware evidence use. LLM agents can analyze data through natural language and tools, but their performance in evolving environments has been largely untested. TimeSage-EV fills this gap by providing a live benchmark with ground truth from withheld target releases.

## [Intern-S2-Mobius: Decoupling Knowledge and Reasoning for Efficient Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14290) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Mobius-v0 architecture separates knowledge storage (FFN) from reasoning (self-attention), enabling a 7B model to match a Transformer baseline with only 62.6% of training data and Intern-S2-Mobius (35B) to achieve nearly 4x inference speedup. This architecture challenges the monolithic design of current foundation models, offering a path to more data-efficient training and faster inference, which could reduce costs and enable deployment in resource-constrained environments. The 7B model trained from scratch matches a 7B Transformer baseline with 62.6% of training data, while Intern-S2-Mobius, continually pretrained from Qwen3.5-35B, delivers similar downstream scores with nearly 4x end-to-end inference speedup. The architecture uses hidden states as cache and carrier for knowledge queries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional Transformer models interleave knowledge storage and reasoning within each layer, which can be inefficient. Mobius-v0 decouples these functions: a shared memory (FFN) stores knowledge vectors, while multiple reasoners (self-attention) iteratively query the memory to perform compositional reasoning.

## [Wrong Answers Can Guide Multi-Agent Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14375) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Diverse Hypothesis Deliberation (DHD), a protocol to measure the trajectory value of messages in multi-agent reasoning, showing that wrong answers can still be helpful for downstream reasoning. Across five benchmarks and two model families, wrong-helpful messages appear in every combination, with over 40% of wrong-answer messages that change final correctness being helpful. This challenges the common assumption that correctness equals usefulness in multi-agent systems, potentially influencing how agents filter and prioritize messages. It provides a new measurement protocol and reusable labels for learning when agents should listen, which could improve reasoning optimization and system design. The DHD protocol caches five independently generated messages and replays the same downstream solver with each message available or hidden to measure trajectory value. Controlled repeats show that the number of repeatable message effects is unlikely to arise from replay variation alone (p=0.0002), and a focused intervention finds that the complete message works best, with reasoning preserving more success than answer alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent reasoning systems often use agreement, confidence, or automated scores to decide which messages should shape the final answer, assuming that correct messages are worth keeping. However, a wrong answer can contain useful decompositions, constraints, or scientific principles. The DHD protocol provides a controlled way to measure whether a message helps or harms subsequent reasoning, independent of its correctness.

## [Tripwire: Training-Free Jailbreak Defense via Safety Neuron Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14392) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Tripwire is a training-free defense that identifies safety-specific neurons via per-neuron hypothesis tests with false-discovery-rate control and a utility-specificity filter, then applies a trigger-style clamp to induce refusal behavior. It reduces average attack success rate to at most 2.0% while incurring only 0.5% to 5.3% utility drop on MT-Bench across four LLMs and four attacks. This addresses key limitations of existing neuron-level defenses, which often compromise model utility or perturb benign requests. By offering a training-free, targeted intervention, Tripwire could enhance LLM security without significant performance trade-offs, benefiting developers and users of safety-aligned models. The method uses a trigger-style clamp that holds selected neurons at their harmful-conditional mean activations, injecting an internal harmful-input signal to trigger refusal. It offers two deployment modes: detector-gated inference-time intervention and offline bias-patch weight edit, both provably equivalent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Jailbreak attacks manipulate LLMs to produce outputs violating safety alignment, often bypassing RLHF-based training. Neuron-level interventions aim to defend by modifying internal representations, but existing methods either suppress toxic neurons broadly (hurting utility) or rely on external classifiers that may compromise important neurons. Tripwire uses statistical testing to pinpoint safety-specific neurons, enabling a more precise and less invasive defense.

## [AI Scientists: From Adam and Eve to Integrated Autonomous Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14407) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This survey reviews the past and future of AI Scientists, machines that automate the entire scientific process, highlighting the historical systems Adam and Eve and the current challenge of integrating neural learning with autonomous experimentation. It also references the Nobel Turing Challenge, which aims to achieve Nobel-quality discoveries by 2050. AI Scientists have the potential to make science faster, cheaper, more systematic, and more reproducible, and could tackle problems too complex for human scientists alone. This survey is significant because it frames the central problem as integration, not automation of individual components, which is crucial for advancing autonomous scientific discovery. Adam was the first machine to make novel scientific discoveries through cycles of hypothesis formation and physical experimentation, while Eve established the architecture of the modern self-driving laboratory. The survey argues that foundation models, autonomous agents, and laboratory robotics now enable more general systems, but the key challenge is combining neural learning with logic, probability, mathematics, causal reasoning, simulation, experimental design, robotics, and formal scientific records.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: AI Scientists are integrated scientific agents that can originate hypotheses, deduce consequences, design and execute experiments, interpret results, and revise beliefs. The concept builds on earlier robot scientists like Adam and Eve, which were developed at Aberystwyth University for yeast functional genomics and drug screening, respectively. Self-driving laboratories, which automate experimentation using AI and robotics, are a key component of this vision. The Nobel Turing Challenge sets a goal for AI systems to achieve Nobel-quality discoveries by 2050, and progress is reportedly ahead of schedule.

## [OptStop: Bayesian Adaptive Stopping Cuts LLM Evaluation Compute by 57-97%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14425) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces optstop, a precision-based adaptive stopping framework for LLM evaluations that uses hierarchical Bayesian inference to decide when to stop sampling. In an illustrative 200-item, 10-epoch evaluation, it removes 57%-97% of planned trials across nine validation settings while maintaining equivalent conclusions. LLM evaluations are computationally expensive, and fixed sampling budgets waste resources on items whose estimates are already precise. By allocating compute based on uncertainty, optstop can significantly reduce evaluation costs, making it easier to conduct large-scale or frequent evaluations, which is timely given the high cost of LLM evaluations. The framework supports binary, ordinal, and continuous outcomes, and does not require a calibrated item bank. It can run live or retrospectively, and includes a safeguard that samples more cautiously as measured performance approaches zero, where rare successes matter most.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: LLM evaluations typically use fixed sampling budgets, testing every item the same number of times even after estimates are precise. optstop treats evaluation as a sequential measurement problem, building on hierarchical Bayesian inference, which is a statistical approach that models uncertainty at multiple levels. This approach is related to other works like HiBayES, which also uses hierarchical Bayesian modeling for LLM evaluation, and to optimal stopping theory in sequential experimental design.

## [Toby Ord's New Model Reveals Harder Path to Intelligence Explosion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14426) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Toby Ord's new paper analyzes the mathematics of intelligence explosions, showing that singular growth (towards a vertical asymptote) is harder to achieve than previous economics-inspired models suggested. He identifies generation time—the time to complete one feedback loop—as a critical, previously neglected parameter. This work challenges existing assumptions in AI safety and singularity research, potentially reshaping how we assess the risk of rapid AI capability growth. By highlighting generation time, it offers a new lever for understanding and possibly controlling the dynamics of AI-driven R&D feedback loops. Ord shows that there exists a class of growth rates faster than exponential that do not lead to a vertical asymptote, complicating the picture of explosive growth. He concludes that singular growth cannot occur unless generation time rapidly approaches zero, making it a pivotal factor in any intelligence explosion scenario.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: An intelligence explosion is a hypothetical scenario, first proposed by I.J. Good in 1965, where an AI capable of improving itself leads to a rapid, uncontrollable growth in intelligence. The technological singularity is the broader event where such growth surpasses human control. Ord's paper uses mathematical modeling to explore the dynamics of such feedback loops, building on but also critiquing recent economics-inspired approaches.

## [PACE-Bench: New Benchmark for Physics Adaptation via Code Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14441) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PACE-Bench is a new benchmark introduced in arXiv:2608.14441, consisting of 144 source-to-target adaptation pairs across six physics domains, designed to evaluate self-evolving agents' ability to adapt code to mutated physics environments. The benchmark shows that current methods are far from solving it, with the best method (Reflexion + Qwen3-14B) succeeding on only 35.9% of full-benchmark pairs. This benchmark addresses a significant gap in evaluating self-evolving agents, which typically optimize under fixed conditions and do not test recovery after changes. By providing a challenging, non-saturated benchmark, it pushes the field towards developing more robust adaptation mechanisms, which is crucial for real-world deployment where environments are dynamic. The benchmark includes ten self-evolving methods from four paradigms, and results show that simulator-grounded reflection is more reliable than unverified self-revision, while memory anchors agents to early designs and broad tree search explores without converging. Even revealing exact physical changes does not raise the performance ceiling, pointing to mechanism redesign as the central bottleneck.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Self-evolving agents are AI systems that improve their behavior from interaction experience, but existing evaluations often assume fixed execution conditions. PACE-Bench uses a simulator-grounded approach where agents must iteratively adapt code to mutated physics environments using diagnostic sandbox feedback within a limited attempt budget. This benchmark is a concrete proxy produced by PACE (A Proxy for Agentic Capability Evaluation), and it is available on Hugging Face and GitHub.

The Twin system uses a frontier coding agent to write executable world models for grid games, achieving 97.8% level completion (179/183) on the ARC-AGI-3 benchmark, outperforming humans in efficiency on 88.3% of cleared levels. This demonstrates that test-time world-model inference can dramatically improve agent performance on interactive reasoning benchmarks, potentially shifting AI research toward dynamic model construction rather than static training. It also highlights the importance of goal inference over world modeling, which could influence future continual learning systems. The system infers the goal before any reward on 87.2% of cleared levels, and uses replay validation in a twin world model, enforcing that actions are only taken when the model reproduces all previous transitions. The base model alone scores 7.8%, but with the Twin harness it reaches 93.3%, clearing 23 out of 25 games.

## [Participatory Moral AI Is Not Neutral: The Invisible Hand of Developers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14522) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that developer choices in feature scoping, voter sampling, and question framing significantly shape the moral preferences elicited for AI systems, challenging the neutrality of participatory moral AI. This matters because it reveals that voting-based alignment cannot deliver fair or transparent AI by aggregation alone, highlighting the need for auditing and disclosure at each stage of the moral AI elicitation pipeline. It affects researchers, practitioners, and policymakers involved in AI ethics and alignment. The study involved two phases (N = 809) across three deployment contexts: AI kidney allocation, AI agents simulating absent workers, and generative AI depictions of the deceased. It found that morally relevant features shift across contexts, preferences differ by political ideology for roughly one-third of features, and question wording can narrow or widen ideological gaps by up to a full scale point.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Moral preference elicitation is an approach where researchers poll participants on hypothetical dilemmas and use aggregated votes to train AI policies. This method assumes that aggregating individual preferences can yield fair and transparent AI decisions, but this paper shows that developer choices in the pipeline introduce biases. Participatory design in AI aims to involve stakeholders in the design process, but this study highlights that even participatory methods are not neutral.

## [Benchmark Optimization Doesn't Prove General Coding Ability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13566) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.13566) argues that optimizing for coding benchmarks like SWE-bench and LiveCodeBench does not generalize to broader coding tasks, using a Django-based case study to show limited cross-task transfer. This challenges the common practice of using benchmark scores as evidence of general coding capability, which is widespread in AI research and product claims. The findings could push the community toward more diverse evaluation methods, affecting how models are assessed and deployed. The authors created a Django-based benchmark suite and evaluated models post-trained on SWE-bench trajectories. They found that post-trained checkpoints show little cross-task transfer, and SWE-bench optimization yields limited or no gains on their tasks or LiveCodeBench.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: SWE-bench is a benchmark for evaluating LLMs on real-world GitHub issues, while LiveCodeBench is a contamination-free benchmark that continuously collects new coding problems. The paper argues that a small number of benchmarks is insufficient for evaluating diverse models under optimization pressure, and calls for differentiated evaluation and sustained benchmark maintenance.

## [Study: LSP Semantic Retrieval Often Not Token-Efficient for Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13568) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a measurement methodology and preliminary study comparing token efficiency of LSP-based semantic retrieval versus lexical retrieval (grep) for coding agents, finding that semantic retrieval is often not more token-efficient and can even cost more tokens. This study challenges a common assumption in AI-assisted coding that semantic retrieval via LSP is more token-efficient, providing empirical evidence that could influence how coding agents are designed and how retrieval tools are chosen, potentially leading to more adaptive and cost-effective systems. The study used Python and TypeScript repos with Claude Opus 4.8, Sonnet 4.6, and Haiku 4.5, measuring tokens-to-success across five ablation arms. Results show LSP costs +6% to +118% tokens on symbol-named localization, and on reference-completeness it saves tokens only for the weakest model; on edits, grep solves multi-file renames perfectly while LSP fails 75% of the time.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents rely heavily on retrieving relevant code context, often using lexical tools like grep or semantic tools like LSP. LSP provides precise, typed information but requires a running server and per-symbol round-trips, while grep is fast and universal but noisy. The paper formalizes the token efficiency question with a metric called tokens-to-success and proposes an adaptive router based on task class, model capability, and lexical noise.

## [InflationAgent: Routing LLM Agents by True Workflow Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13571) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces InflationAgent, a four-stage router that accounts for token inflation in agentic LLM workflows, and proposes CoT Branching Entropy (CBE), a pre-execution difficulty signal. On GSM8K, InflationAgent achieves 94.7% accuracy versus 91.0% for FrugalGPT while using 31% fewer tokens. This work addresses a critical cost inefficiency in agentic LLM systems, where retry overhead can make per-token pricing misleading. By routing based on true workflow cost, InflationAgent can significantly reduce real expenses for practitioners deploying multi-step LLM applications. Token inflation is defined as the ratio of true workflow cost to single-call cost, and can be as high as 4.25x for a 7B model on multi-hop QA. The CBE signal predicts high inflation with AUROC 0.887, and forwarding a failed reasoning chain to GPT-4o reduces its accuracy by up to 34.8 percentage points, validating the fresh-escalation design.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic LLM systems often retry failed queries, consuming additional tokens each time, creating a gap between per-token price and actual workflow cost. Traditional routers like FrugalGPT optimize based on single-call cost, which can underestimate real costs by more than 2x on difficult tasks. InflationAgent introduces a new difficulty signal and a routing metric (Semantic Exchange Rate) to address this.

## [Activation-Guided Pruning Enables Training-Free Cross-Scale Model Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13596) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Activation-Prune-Merge (APM), a framework that transfers capabilities from a large donor language model to a smaller recipient without explicit neuron alignment, using activation-guided pruning and micro interpolation. Across 16 benchmarks, APM improves the recipient's average accuracy from 55.5% to 60.6%. This work addresses the underexplored problem of cross-scale heterogeneous model fusion, offering a practical, training-free method to enhance smaller models using larger ones. It could enable efficient model improvement without costly fine-tuning, benefiting resource-constrained deployment scenarios. APM constructs task-conditioned activation maps on the donor, selects salient layers, hidden dimensions, attention heads, and MLP neurons to prune it to the recipient architecture, then injects the donor slice with a micro interpolation coefficient. Notable improvements include RTE accuracy rising from 64.3% to 82.3%, QNLI from 52.3% to 65.7%, and BoolQ from 70.8% to 79.2%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Heterogeneous model fusion aims to combine models that differ in tasks, initializations, architectures, or scales. Traditional methods often require explicit neuron-wise semantic alignment, which is challenging when architectures differ substantially. APM treats the donor as a source of concentrated functional components, avoiding the need for precise structural transplantation.

## [Three-Stage Fine-Tuning of Qwen3-27B for C-to-Rust Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13681) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a three-stage fine-tuning curriculum for Qwen3-27B to improve C-to-Rust code translation, consisting of continued pretraining on Rust corpora, debugging-aware SFT on the Verus_Training_Data dataset, and task-specific SFT on paired C/Rust solutions from LeetCode. The resulting model is evaluated using the SACTOR framework, showing improved success rates and idiomaticity compared to baseline models. C-to-Rust translation is crucial for eliminating memory-safety vulnerabilities in legacy systems, and this work demonstrates a systematic fine-tuning approach that could be applied to other code translation tasks. It also highlights the potential of specialized LLM fine-tuning to achieve practical results in software engineering. The three stages are: (1) continued pretraining on Rust-centric corpora, (2) SFT on microsoft/Verus_Training_Data for debugging and self-repair, and (3) task-specific SFT on LeetCode-derived C/Rust pairs. Evaluation uses SACTOR, which performs structure-aware two-phase translation with FFI-based end-to-end testing, and reports metrics like success rate, Clippy lint counts, and unsafe-code fraction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: C-to-Rust translation is a challenging task because it requires generating idiomatic and safe Rust code that preserves the original C semantics. Traditional transpilers like C2Rust produce unsafe and unidiomatic code, requiring manual refinement. Large language models (LLMs) have shown promise but often underperform off-the-shelf. This paper leverages Qwen3-27B, a 27B-parameter model, and a structured fine-tuning curriculum to address these challenges.

## [MedPlex: Bidirectional Vision-Language Fusion for Medical Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13690) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MedPlex introduces an end-to-end vision-language framework for medical image segmentation that integrates clinical text guidance continuously through bidirectional fusion and multi-level concept alignment. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on CT and MR benchmarks for multi-organ, cardiac substructure, and tumor segmentation. This work addresses a key limitation in existing text-guided segmentation methods, where language is often used only as a late conditioning signal. By making text guidance a continuous component of representation learning, MedPlex could improve the accuracy and clinical relevance of medical image segmentation, benefiting diagnostic and treatment planning workflows. MedPlex uses Bi-Fusion (Bidirectional Fusion) to jointly evolve visual and textual representations across the encoding hierarchy, and introduces class-level and region-level concept alignment. Class-level alignment anchors anatomical targets to aggregated clinical concept profiles, while region-level alignment preserves individual concepts like shape, location, appearance, and texture through class-specific visual evidence.

Vision-Language Models (VLMs) aim to bridge this gap, but many existing methods use language only as a late-stage cue, limiting its influence on visual representation learning. MedPlex addresses this by integrating text guidance throughout the encoder, enabling more clinically grounded segmentation.

## [Amplified Reasoning Behaviors Are Not Predictive of Correctness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13760) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Behavioral Lift, a metric measuring how much correctness changes when a behavior is present versus absent in a reasoning trace, and reveals an Amplification-Lift Gap across 15 models and 6 benchmarks. The study annotates 15,282 traces and finds that thinking models amplify self-correction, hypothesis testing, and uncertainty acknowledgment, while the highest-lift behaviors are confidence calibration, knowledge alignment, and self-awareness. This finding challenges the assumption that reasoning-oriented training improves correctness by amplifying deliberative behaviors, and suggests that current training objectives may reward surface form over substantive reasoning. It has broad implications for LLM/VLM training and evaluation, motivating process-level objectives that reward calibrated and grounded reasoning. The study covers both text-only and vision-language reasoning, with a taxonomy whose core behaviors are defined for both LLM and VLM traces. Notably, uncertainty acknowledgment is amplified by 3–7× yet is weakly or negatively associated with correctness, while confidence calibration is among the strongest positive signals but barely amplified.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Reasoning-oriented training, such as that used in models like DeepSeek-R1, aims to improve performance by encouraging long chains of thought and deliberative behaviors. However, it has been unclear whether these amplified behaviors are actually the ones most predictive of correctness. Behavioral Lift provides a way to quantify this relationship, and the Amplification-Lift Gap highlights a potential misalignment between training objectives and true reasoning quality.

## [PPAPlace: Differentiable Cross-Stage Objectives for Chip Placement Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13790) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PPAPlace introduces a timing-driven differentiable surrogate that predicts post-route PPA from macro and standard-cell placements, trained on post-global-routing labels. It improves average WNS and TNS by 22% and 51% over the hierarchical baseline on five ChiPBench test circuits. This work addresses the critical issue that HPWL correlates poorly with post-route timing, which undermines AI-based placement methods. By providing differentiable timing objectives, PPAPlace enables more effective optimization of chip PPA, potentially advancing AI-driven chip design. The surrogate uses a dual-stream architecture combining graph attention over the netlist and spatial convolution over the placement grid. PPAPlace operates in two modes: CoOpt injects timing objectives into an analytical placer's loop, while Refine adjusts macro positions via projected gradient descent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Chip placement is a critical step in VLSI physical design, determining module locations on a chip. Traditional methods optimize half-perimeter wirelength (HPWL) as a proxy for wirelength, but recent benchmarks show HPWL correlates poorly with post-route timing metrics like worst negative slack (WNS) and total negative slack (TNS). This paper proposes a differentiable surrogate trained on post-global-routing labels to bridge the gap between placement objectives and final PPA.

## [ASSERT Pipeline Ties GenAI Audit Rates to Measurement Choices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13840) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced ASSERT, a specification-driven measurement pipeline for GenAI audits that links each reported compliance rate to an explicit written specification of the measurement choices. In a case study on conversational deception, they found that the reported rate varies substantially with dialogue setup, simulated user, judge, and evidence bar, and can even reorder system rankings. This work addresses a critical reproducibility and interpretability issue in GenAI auditing, where reported rates are often ambiguous and hard to compare across audits. By making measurement choices explicit, ASSERT helps stakeholders better attribute differences in audit results, supporting more reliable system comparisons and deployment decisions. ASSERT assists in drafting a behavioral rubric and test cases, then runs the audit and returns a reported rate tied to the specification. The case study on conversational deception demonstrates that measurement choices can substantially change the reported rate and reorder GenAI system rankings, highlighting the practical implications of the pipeline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: GenAI audits often summarize system behavior as a compliance rate, which is used to compare systems and gate deployment. However, these rates depend on many measurement choices, such as how the test is set up and how compliance is judged, making it unclear whether changes in the rate reflect the system or the measurement. ASSERT aims to solve this by requiring a written specification of these choices, improving transparency and interpretability.

## [Engineering Reliable Coding Agents: A System-Level Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13867) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This monograph presents a comprehensive framework for evaluating and operating AI coding agents as systems, synthesizing 164 scholarly works, 100 practitioner records, 29 benchmark records, and 17 author-system case records. It introduces a versioned catalog of 206 reliability records, including 193 gated practices and 13 research leads, along with runnable evaluation protocols and five reusable agent skills. This work addresses a critical gap between model-centric evaluation and system-level deployment, highlighting that many apparent model failures actually originate in the harness, execution state, retrieval, or other system components. It provides practitioners and researchers with a methodology to distinguish model capability from infrastructure effects, enabling more reliable and defensible coding agent systems. The monograph treats evaluation and operation as a dependency chain, where weaknesses in task construction, execution environments, retrieval, state management, verification, or observability can invalidate downstream conclusions. It also provides an evidence ledger, a framework for dependency and repair asymmetry, and measurements from operated agent systems, while noting that the review is structured rather than exhaustive and evidence strength varies by topic.

**Background**: AI coding agents are typically evaluated as models but deployed as systems, meaning their reliability depends on the entire system around the model, including the harness, execution state, retrieval, memory, permissions, and resource allocation. The concept of an 'agent harness' refers to the software infrastructure that enables an LLM to operate as an agent, managing tool use, memory, and feedback loops. This monograph uses a multivocal literature review method, which synthesizes evidence from both peer-reviewed and grey literature, to provide a comprehensive system-level perspective.

The paper introduces the concept of 'agentic transactions' and proposes an ACID-compliant framework for LLM agents, reinterpreting ACID properties as Semantic Atomicity, Consistency, Isolation, and Durability. It also presents an ACID-compliant data agent that achieves a 10.6% improvement over state-of-the-art agents like Claude Code on benchmarks. This work addresses critical reliability and consistency issues in autonomous LLM agents, bridging database systems and AI agents. It offers a principled approach that could lead to more trustworthy and scalable agent systems, impacting industries relying on multi-step AI workflows. The framework is instantiated through transactional exploration-execution-validation cycles, transactional skill hubs, confidence divergence-based validation, semantic dependency-aware isolation, and transaction-aware semantic state management. The paper is a preprint (arXiv:2608.13900) and has not yet undergone peer review.

Domain-adversarial learning reduces embodiment-specific information in visual representations, and experiments show data-efficient few-shot adaptation and generalization to unseen objects and environments.

HAM-RAG improves multimodal average by 17.3% over the strongest non-hierarchical baseline across multiple backbones, and on Wukong it improves Img-CBS by 24.2%. ## [TransTS: Reaction-Aware Flow Matching for Generalizable Transition State Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14076) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces TransTS, a reaction-transformation-aware framework that explicitly learns atom-level structural transformations between reactants and products and integrates them with a unified atom-aligned geometric representation to generate transition state geometries via flow matching. It demonstrates improved TS initialization quality and stronger generalization to unseen reaction distributions on IID and zero-shot OOD benchmarks, including GDB-10-rxn and GDB-17-rxn. Transition state generation is a critical bottleneck in computational chemistry, and TransTS addresses a key limitation of existing ML methods by explicitly modeling structural transformations, leading to more reliable TS initial guesses that converge to validated saddle points. This advancement could accelerate reaction mechanism discovery and enable more efficient quantum-chemical refinement, benefiting fields like drug discovery and materials science. TransTS uses flow matching, a simulation-free generative modeling paradigm, and is designed to provide reliable TS initial guesses for subsequent quantum-chemical refinement. The generated structures are evaluated not only by geometric similarity but also by their ability to converge to validated saddle points and recover intended reaction pathways, with scaling reaction coverage and model capacity further improving outcomes.

**Background**: Transition states (TS) are saddle points on the potential energy surface that define reaction barriers and mechanisms, but finding them requires expensive quantum-mechanical calculations. Recent machine learning approaches predict TS geometries from reactant and product endpoints, but often rely on implicit geometric correspondence. Atom mapping, which establishes correspondences between atoms in reactants and products, is crucial for understanding reaction mechanisms and is a prerequisite for learning atom-level transformations. Flow matching is a recent generative modeling technique that trains continuous normalizing flows by regressing vector fields, offering a scalable and simulation-free alternative to diffusion models.

## [Forecast Collapse in Time-Series Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14106) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper identifies a new phenomenon called 'forecast collapse' in time-series foundation models, where predictions become nearly flat and poorly ranked for low-predictability targets, and introduces CalibRank to address the calibration-ranking tradeoff. This finding reveals a blind spot in conventional time-series evaluation, as per-series metrics can hide failures in cross-series structure needed for downstream decisions. It has significant implications for financial forecasting and any application relying on cross-sectional ranking. The study analyzes forecast collapse across time-series foundation models, twelve deep-learning forecasting models, and 97 public benchmark configurations, finding it closely tied to target predictability. CalibRank nearly triples cross-sectional correlation on Finance1K while keeping amplitude close to the target, and improves correlation on all tested models.

## [Transferable Latent Operator Bridges Eulerian and Lagrangian Fluid Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14120) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes the Transferable Latent Operator (TLO), a neural operator that learns a unified flow representation from Eulerian fields and can generalize zero-shot to Lagrangian particle rollout without Lagrangian supervision. TLO decouples latent flow evolution from coordinate-dependent decoding, enabling querying at fixed coordinates for Eulerian fields and at particle positions for Lagrangian rollout. This work addresses a significant gap in neural operators, which are typically trained and evaluated only on Eulerian fields, while Lagrangian trajectories are often unavailable. By enabling zero-shot transfer from Eulerian to Lagrangian representations, TLO could broaden the applicability of neural operators in fluid dynamics and scientific machine learning, potentially reducing the need for expensive Lagrangian data collection. TLO consistently outperforms existing neural operators across five fluid-dynamics benchmarks in both Eulerian field prediction and zero-shot Lagrangian rollout, with further gains from limited Lagrangian fine-tuning. The architecture decouples latent flow evolution from coordinate-dependent decoding, allowing the same latent representation to be queried at different coordinate types.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: In fluid dynamics, Eulerian and Lagrangian are two complementary descriptions: Eulerian fields track properties at fixed spatial points, while Lagrangian trajectories track individual fluid particles over time. Neural operators learn mappings between function spaces and are designed to be discretization-invariant, but most existing models are trained and evaluated only on Eulerian data. TLO aims to bridge these two representations by learning a unified latent flow representation that can be queried in either coordinate system.

The paper introduces XIGL, an architecture-agnostic human-in-the-loop strategy that combines explanation analysis with active learning to identify and mitigate shortcut learning in Graph Neural Networks (GNNs). It supports any query strategy and includes a proposed active learning approach that prioritizes explanations likely to display shortcut behavior, reducing annotation costs. Shortcut learning compromises the reliability of GNNs in out-of-distribution tasks, a significant problem in real-world applications. XIGL offers a practical solution that leverages human expertise to deconfound models, potentially improving the robustness and trustworthiness of GNNs across various domains. XIGL is architecture-agnostic and supports any query strategy, but the authors develop a specific active learning strategy to prioritize explanations more likely to show shortcut behavior. The method is showcased on several GNN architectures, and the implementation is available online.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

Shortcut learning is a known issue in deep neural networks, where models rely on spurious correlations instead of underlying causal mechanisms. Human-in-the-loop machine learning integrates human expertise into the model lifecycle, and active learning helps prioritize data for annotation, reducing costs.

## [Self-Supervised Visual On-Policy Distillation Boosts Small VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14144) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Self-Supervised Visual On-Policy Distillation (S2VOPD), which creates learning signals by subtracting information from the student (using asymmetric augmented views) rather than adding privileged information to the teacher. On six fine-grained perception benchmarks, S2VOPD improves Qwen3.5-4B from 70.7% to 77.4%, surpassing all open-source models compared, including Qwen3-VL at 235B, and even GPT-5.4, while recovering 96% of the improvement achieved by privileged-information methods. This work challenges the conventional reliance on privileged information in knowledge distillation, offering a label-free and teacher-free alternative that could democratize distillation for smaller models. It has significant implications for reinforcement learning and robotics, where privileged supervision is often unavailable, and could lead to more efficient and scalable model training in real-world applications. The method systematically explores a broad design space of visual augmentations, finding that asymmetry matters (all four augmentation families improve performance, while symmetric self-distillation degrades it), strength matters (performance peaks at moderate strength), and the gap must remain task-consistent (augmentations that remove question-relevant evidence induce large but uninformative discrepancies). The paper is a preprint (arXiv:2608.14144) and includes a website with additional resources.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation typically transfers knowledge from a larger, stronger teacher model to a smaller student model, often relying on privileged information such as ground-truth labels or reference answers. On-policy distillation provides dense supervision for multimodal reasoners by aligning the student's predictions with the teacher's on-policy outputs. Self-supervised learning aims to create learning signals from the data itself without external labels, and this work applies that principle to distillation by using asymmetric augmented views of the same image to create a teacher-student asymmetry.

## [Concept Guidance: Training-Free Precise Control for Text-to-Image Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14172) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Concept Guidance (CoG) is a novel training-free method that precisely controls text-to-image diffusion models by reinforcing concept-relevant layers, improving local coherence without additional training, external models, gradients, or prompt engineering. It introduces concept-wise mutual information to identify and exploit layer-specific contributions, demonstrating performance gains on models like PixArt-alpha, SD3, SD3.5, and FLUX.1-dev. This method addresses two major limitations in text-to-image diffusion models: lack of continuous, concept-specific guidance and poor local coherence for tasks like generating text or hands. By providing a training-free, out-of-the-box solution, CoG could significantly enhance the practical utility and reliability of generative AI across various applications. CoG quantifies each layer's concept-specific impact and guides denoising using a weighted combination of predictions generated with concept-relevant layers skipped. The method is model-agnostic and requires only a custom pipeline after profiling, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-image diffusion models generate images from textual prompts but often struggle with precise control over specific concepts and maintaining local coherence. Traditional guidance methods may require additional training or external models, while CoG leverages the observation that different concepts are localized in distinct network layers, enabling targeted intervention without extra resources.
