## [SAFARI: Instilling Proactive Exploration in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14339) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SAFARI, a method to instill and refine proactive exploration in LLM agents, consisting of Exploratory Data Construction and RL Optimization with Contrastive Signal Guidance. Experiments demonstrate its effectiveness, and code is available on GitHub. Proactive exploration is a crucial capability for LLM agents to acquire information for better decision-making, addressing a gap in current agent training. This work could improve agent performance in complex, interactive environments, benefiting the broader AI agent community. SAFARI mitigates hindsight bias in standard demonstrations by synthesizing exploration-rich trajectories, and uses contrastive trajectory pairs to distinguish productive exploration from redundant wandering. The method is validated through extensive experiments, with code available at https://github.com/GuanZhizhao/SAFARI.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are AI systems that use large language models to perform tasks in interactive environments. Proactive exploration refers to the agent's ability to actively seek information that improves future decisions, which is often hindered by hindsight bias in training data and a lack of clear signals for productive exploration. Reinforcement learning (RL) is a common method to train such agents, and contrastive learning techniques can help distinguish between useful and redundant behaviors.

## [Disentangled Shared Representations Improve Morpho-Transcriptomic Integration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14355) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates whether explicitly disentangling shared and private latent components improves multimodal representation learning for paired H&E and spatial transcriptomics data. It compares VAE-based and contrastive approaches, each in standard and disentangled variants, across two cancer cohorts. This work addresses a key limitation in multimodal representation learning for spatial transcriptomics, where standard models often compress modalities without separating shared and specific variations. The findings could guide the development of more effective foundation models for integrating morphology and gene expression, benefiting downstream analyses in cancer research. The study uses two cancer cohorts and evaluates representations using cross-modal reconstruction, downstream probing, and cross-modal probe transfer. Results show that contrastive objectives yield higher downstream probing performance than VAE-based models, and disentangled variants improve selected metrics, though gains depend on model family, task, direction, and disentanglement strength.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Spatial transcriptomics (ST) enables simultaneous profiling of gene expression and tissue morphology, creating opportunities for multimodal learning. Multimodal representation learning projects different modalities into a shared latent space, while disentangled representation learning aims to separate latent factors into independent components. This paper combines these concepts to improve integration of H&E images and ST data.

## [LLMs Cannot Perform the Abductive 'Jump': A Thermodynamic Coupling Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14397) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper argues that large language models (LLMs) cannot perform the abductive 'Jump' exemplified by Planck's quantum postulate, E = hν, and proposes that this limitation stems from a lack of coupling between epistemic error and physical cost. The authors formalize this distinction through thermodynamic coupling and show that fixed-weight transformer inference lacks such coupling, regardless of model scale. This work challenges the prevailing assumption that scaling up LLMs will eventually enable human-like scientific discovery, suggesting that a fundamental architectural change may be needed. It contributes to the ongoing debate about the limits of AI in abductive reasoning, with implications for AI research and philosophy of science. The paper cites empirical results showing that output entropy remains nearly unchanged across tasks with sharply increasing causal difficulty, even as accuracy falls from 100% to 17%. It also engages with prior work by Zahavy (2026) on embodied simulation and responses by Zheng-Xin (2026) and Farmer (2026) questioning the necessity of embodiment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Abductive reasoning is the process of inferring the most plausible explanation from observations, a key aspect of scientific discovery. Planck's quantum postulate, introduced in 1900, resolved the blackbody radiation problem by proposing that energy is quantized, a leap that could not be derived from induction or deduction alone. The paper argues that this 'Jump' required a coupling between epistemic error and physical cost, which fixed-weight transformer inference lacks.

SheetCompass introduces a graph-guided, memory-driven agentic framework that explicitly models structural relationships within and across worksheets to improve LLM reasoning over complex workbooks. By enabling LLMs to understand spreadsheet structures better, it could lead to more accurate and efficient automation of data manipulation tasks, benefiting analysts and non-experts alike. SheetCompass uses hierarchical relation graphs to model intra-sheet and inter-sheet relationships, and maintains task-relevant information in memory for incremental graph traversal. This design minimizes information loss and allows agents to reason over complex workbooks more effectively than existing flattening methods.

## [Formalizing Session Handover as In-Context Learning State Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14528) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper formalizes session handover in LLM applications as the transfer of a task-relative in-context learning (ICL) state, providing theoretical conditions and bit requirements for sufficient handover. It distinguishes exact recovery from preservation of the target distribution and proposes a three-part record for practical handover. This work addresses a practical problem in LLM applications—session handover—with a rigorous theoretical framework, which can guide engineers in deciding what information to retain when context limits or agent changes occur. It bridges theory and practice, potentially improving the reliability and efficiency of LLM-based systems. Under an exogeneity condition, predictive equivalence characterizes the coarsest deterministic sufficient handover and gives a fixed-length bit requirement. The analysis isolates effects of memory constraint, writer, and continuation procedure, and quantifies the cost of writing before the downstream query is known. Gaussian linear regression yields exact finite-dimensional handover and finite-bit perturbation bounds, while nonparametric regression provides upper and lower bounds relating memory to squared prediction error.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: In-context learning (ICL) is a capability of large language models where the model performs a task by conditioning on input-output examples without parameter updates. Session handover occurs when a task continues in a new session due to context limits, restarts, or agent changes, requiring the application to decide which information to pass on. Exogeneity is a statistical assumption that independent variables are not dependent on the dependent variable, which is used here to ensure predictive equivalence.

## [SELR: Self-Explainable Latent Reasoning Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13570) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SELR, a unified framework that trains a single model to perform latent reasoning while simultaneously decoding its own latent representations into human-readable reasoning steps, eliminating the need for external decoders. It validates the approach on both LLMs and VLMs, achieving superior token efficiency and accuracy compared to baselines. SELR addresses the critical trade-off between efficiency and interpretability in latent reasoning, offering a path to models that are both fast and transparent. This could influence future LLM reasoning research and applications where explainability is crucial, such as in healthcare or finance. The core contribution is a multi-task training objective combining an Answer Loss for task accuracy and a CoT Loss for decoding latent representations into reasoning steps. The framework is validated on both LLMs and VLMs, showing superior token efficiency and accuracy while providing self-contained explainability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Latent reasoning compresses verbose chain-of-thought reasoning into compact embeddings, improving efficiency but often making the reasoning opaque. Existing methods either lack interpretability (e.g., Coconut) or rely on separate decoders (e.g., Heima), which adds overhead and decouples explanation from actual reasoning. SELR aims to unify these aspects in a single model.

The Architect is a new system that converts Microsoft Excel into an interactive tool for visualizing the full forward and backward passes of neural networks. Users describe a network in a compact table, and the system generates a workbook with live spreadsheet formulas for computations, while inputs, weights, labels, and hyperparameters remain editable. This bridges the gap between high-level architecture diagrams and low-level code, making deep learning mathematics more accessible to educators and researchers. It could enhance teaching and debugging of neural networks by providing an inspectable, interactive medium that leverages Excel's familiar interface. The system generates aligned PyTorch snippets to connect formulas with implementation, and supports use cases like arithmetic tracing, learning-rate exploration, diagnosing dying ReLU, and inspecting vanishing gradients. It relies on Excel's recalculation engine for reactive updates of dependent computations.

## [BCMT: Blockwise Causal Memory Transformer for Efficient Long-Context Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13578) ⭐️ 7.0/10

BCMT introduces a novel architecture that decouples local self-attention from global context propagation using a parallelizable exponential causal memory, achieving comparable validation performance to dense Transformers while improving training throughput and reducing memory consumption. This addresses the quadratic complexity limitation of standard Transformers, enabling more efficient long-context language modeling. The approach could influence future efficient attention designs and benefit applications requiring long sequences, such as document understanding and code generation. The memory mechanism is fully parallelizable and compatible with standard dense self-attention implementations. Experiments were conducted with context lengths up to 1024 tokens, and an ablation study confirms the improvements stem from the proposed memory mechanism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Transformer architectures rely on dense self-attention, which has quadratic complexity with respect to sequence length, limiting their efficiency for long contexts. BCMT decouples local token interactions from global context propagation by applying dense causal self-attention within local blocks and using an exponential causal memory to propagate global context, avoiding both dense interactions between distant tokens and learned memory states.

## [IterCOMP: Training-Free Reasoning-Aware Prompt Compression for Multi-hop QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13588) ⭐️ 7.0/10

IterCOMP is a new training-free prompt compression framework that integrates multi-hop reasoning into an iterative compression loop, decomposing documents into evidence segments and generating follow-up questions to iteratively integrate essential evidence. Experiments on MusiQue, 2WikiMultiHopQA, and HotpotQA show substantial improvements in Exact Match and F1 scores while reducing token budget. This work addresses a key limitation of existing prompt compression methods, which are typically designed for single-turn queries and fail to capture interdependent reasoning steps in multi-hop QA. By improving both accuracy and efficiency, IterCOMP could enhance RAG systems' performance on complex reasoning tasks, benefiting applications that require multi-step inference. IterCOMP is training-free, meaning it does not require additional fine-tuning of language models. It decomposes documents into evidence segments, evaluates question answerability, and generates targeted follow-up questions to iteratively integrate essential evidence, producing a compact, reasoning-oriented prompt. The method demonstrates robustness as reasoning complexity increases.

**Background**: Multi-hop question answering (QA) requires reasoning across multiple evidence segments, often overwhelming retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems with lengthy and noisy contexts. Prompt compression is an optimization technique that reduces the size of the input prompt while preserving essential information. Existing compression methods are typically designed for single-turn queries and fail to handle the interdependent reasoning steps in multi-hop QA.

## [InvCISD: Invertible Diffusion Framework for Coverless Image Steganography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13597) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes InvCISD, an invertible diffusion framework that couples the latent representations of a secret image and an irrelevant reference image using an invertible network called LIMNet, achieving high visual dissimilarity between secret and stego images while maintaining recovery fidelity. This addresses a critical security vulnerability in existing diffusion-based coverless image steganography (CIS) methods, which often produce stego images that are visually similar to the secret image, potentially exposing hidden information. The proposed framework could enhance the security of image steganography in privacy-sensitive applications. The method trains LIMNet in diffusion latent space, followed by end-to-end fine-tuning of the entire network, including diffusion inversion and generation modules. Experiments show that InvCISD substantially reduces secret-stego visual similarity, improves stego quality, and retains satisfactory secret reconstruction quality, but all evaluated methods remain highly detectable by a CIS-oriented steganalysis model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Coverless image steganography (CIS) synthesizes a stego image without modifying an existing cover, allowing authorized recipients to reconstruct the secret image. Traditional steganography hides data by modifying a cover image, while CIS avoids this by generating a new image, often using generative models like diffusion models. However, existing diffusion-based CIS methods often produce stego images that retain visual similarity to the secret, posing security risks.

This paper introduces a semantic-aware mixed-effects regression framework for evaluating fairness in large audio language models (LALMs), explicitly accounting for semantic variation and speaker identity as confounders. The method uses the model's own embeddings for semantic control, reducing spurious fairness findings on simulated and real-world benchmarks. As LALMs are increasingly deployed for audio understanding tasks, ensuring fair performance across demographic subgroups is critical. This work provides a more robust methodology for bias estimation, potentially influencing how fairness is measured and mitigated in audio AI systems. The framework incorporates sentence-level semantic embeddings of reference text as covariates and models speaker identity as a random effect. Experiments on simulated data and real-world benchmarks demonstrate that the approach substantially reduces spurious fairness findings and yields more robust and interpretable estimates of subgroup performance differences.

**Background**: Mixed-effects models are statistical models that include both fixed and random effects, useful for data with grouped structures like repeated measurements. In fairness evaluation, confounding factors such as semantic content and speaker characteristics can distort bias estimates. This paper leverages mixed-effects regression to control for these confounders, using the model's own embeddings to capture semantic variation as perceived by the model.

## [CLAIR-Fin: Nine-Agent Framework for Claim-Level Verification in Financial QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13706) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CLAIR-Fin introduces a nine-agent framework that decomposes financial questions into atomic claims, verified through Asymmetric Evidence Authority and Chain-of-Custody Verification, with an Adaptive Rebuttal Cycle for contested claims. On the BB-FinQA-X benchmark, it improves faithfulness from 0.780 to 0.889 over a single-pass RAG baseline, while abstaining on 5.4% of questions with insufficient evidence. This framework addresses a critical gap in hallucination detection for multi-agent and retrieval-augmented pipelines, moving verification from the final output to individual claims and agent handoffs. It could improve reliability of AI systems in high-stakes domains like finance, where unsupported answers can have serious consequences. The framework uses a typed Financial Claim Ledger to maintain atomic claims, and a continuous Hallucination Risk Index distinguishes passed claims from those never contested. It outperforms stronger retrieval baselines like HyDE and Graph-RAG on faithfulness (≤0.874), and the evaluation set BB-FinQA-X is built from Bangladesh Bank Annual Report material, stratified by query type, format, and difficulty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) and multi-agent systems often suffer from hallucination, where models generate plausible but unsupported content. Existing defenses typically verify the final aggregate output, missing errors in intermediate claims. CLAIR-Fin addresses this by verifying each claim at agent handoffs and using adversarial debate to resolve contested claims, inspired by legal concepts like chain of custody and asymmetric evidence trust.

## [TeachMateGPT: Multi-Agent Framework Boosts Science Assessment Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13708) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TeachMateGPT introduces a multi-agent, knowledge-grounded framework for generating science assessments from curriculum materials, featuring a hierarchical knowledge base (COPE), a staged fail-closed retrieval pipeline, a source-attributed verification protocol (SAVER), and a new dataset (NCTB-SciGen8). It improves faithfulness from 0.68 to 0.96 and answer relevancy from 0.60 to 0.89 over a vanilla RAG baseline. This work addresses critical limitations in existing RAG systems for educational content, such as flat retrieval and lack of safeguards against weak evidence, potentially reducing teachers' workload and improving the quality of generated assessments. It also contributes to the broader trend of multi-agent RAG systems, which are gaining traction for complex, domain-specific tasks. The framework includes COPE, a hierarchical knowledge base that segments documents along syllabus structure and links them via a graph-based lineage at three granularities. The pipeline uses a coverage gate to withhold generation on insufficient evidence, and SAVER applies stricter grounding checks across each creative question's four sub-parts, with teacher-in-the-loop evaluation. The NCTB-SciGen8 dataset contains 198 items spanning all 14 chapters of the NCTB Class 8 science textbook, rated by three practicing teachers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) combines large language models with external knowledge retrieval to improve factual accuracy. Traditional RAG systems often use flat retrieval and one-shot generate, which can be insufficient for domain-specific tasks like educational assessment generation. Multi-agent RAG systems, such as MA-RAG, orchestrate multiple specialized agents to handle different stages of the pipeline, improving robustness and reasoning. This paper applies such concepts to science education, addressing the need for curriculum-grounded, low-resource settings.

Researchers introduced WitnessSim, a deposition simulator driven by controllable legal personas, and proposed an evaluation framework that separates behavioral realism from pedagogical usefulness. The system was assessed through adversarial testing, blinded attorney comparison, and longitudinal behavioral trajectory analysis, showing plausible behavior and responsiveness to attorney interventions. This work addresses an underexplored area in legal AI evaluation, which has largely focused on factual accuracy and reasoning rather than behavioral realism. It could improve legal training by providing realistic witnesses for deposition practice and offers a framework for evaluating AI simulations in other high-stakes domains. The evaluation framework separates behavioral realism from pedagogical usefulness, and the study found that attorneys did not systematically prefer original testimony over WitnessSim-generated testimony. The system maintained plausible behavioral boundaries and showed meaningful changes in response to question form and attorney intervention, without collapsing the assigned persona.

## [AI-Assisted Development Cost Study Corrected to ~9.9x Ratio](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13730) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A case study on AI-intensive software development initially reported a 19.4x cost ratio, but after correcting two measurement errors, the figure was revised to ~9.9x. The study highlights the importance of accurate cost measurement in AI-assisted development. This study provides early empirical data on the true cost of AI-assisted development, which is crucial for organizations deciding whether to adopt such tools. The correction process reveals common pitfalls in cost estimation that could mislead decision-makers if not addressed. The study used a three-layer cost model including real AI spend, self-reported human effort, and a human counterfactual. The two errors were inferring per-token cost under a flat-rate subscription and using incorrect regional labor rates for the counterfactual, which together inflated the ratio by roughly 2x.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: AI-assisted software development uses tools like large language models to help programmers write code, often via retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems that retrieve relevant documents to answer queries. Measuring the true cost of such development is complex because it involves not only direct AI expenses but also human effort and the hypothetical cost of doing the work without AI. This study is among the first to attempt a rigorous, replicable costing methodology for AI-intensive development.

## [CutClean: Privacy-Aware Pruning Reduces Attribute Leakage in Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13773) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CutClean is a new privacy-aware structured pruning method that reduces private attribute leakage in neural networks while increasing sparsity. It uses auxiliary linear privacy heads attached to intermediate blocks to quantify information leakage and applies increasing sparsity levels to remove it. This addresses a critical privacy concern in high-stakes AI applications, showing that leakage can occur even without traditional dataset biases. It offers a practical method to enhance privacy without sacrificing target accuracy, which is valuable for deploying models that process sensitive attributes. The method uses auxiliary linear privacy heads at each network block to measure leakage, and applies increasing sparsity to remove leakage, measured by the accuracy of the privacy head attached to the last block. Experiments on synthetic and real-world datasets show it effectively minimizes private information flow while achieving high sparsity and preserving classification accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Neural network pruning is a technique that removes redundant parameters or structures to reduce model size and computational cost. Privacy leakage in neural networks refers to the unintended exposure of sensitive attributes (e.g., gender, race) through model outputs or intermediate representations. Traditional methods often rely on dataset biases, but CutClean addresses leakage that occurs independently of such biases.

## [LLM Chatbots Vary in Medical Study Retrieval, Biased Toward Large Trials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13786) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study evaluated three LLM chatbots (Claude Sonnet 5, Gemini 3.1 Pro, and ChatGPT GPT-5.5) on retrieving clinical studies for medical questions, benchmarking against Cochrane review sets. It found that ChatGPT achieved the highest recall (63.1%) and that sample size was the only significant predictor of retrieval. This research highlights significant variability in LLM chatbots' ability to retrieve relevant clinical evidence, which is critical for clinical decision support. The bias toward larger trials could skew evidence-based medicine if these tools are used uncritically. The study used 20 review questions from the 2026 Cochrane Database, simulating patient, clinician, and researcher roles with four repetitions each, yielding 720 responses. On average, chatbots retrieved 39.2% of included studies and cited 5.0% of excluded studies, with sample size showing an odds ratio of 1.80 per log-unit increase.

## [Optimal Power Allocation and AI Receiver for Superimposed DMRS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13809) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes an analytical framework to optimize power allocation and pilot patterns for superimposed DMRS and data transmission in MIMO-OFDM systems, and introduces an AI-based iterative receiver using Transformer encoders. Simulation results show that the proposed AI-ICED receiver with SI-DMRS improves spectral efficiency compared to conventional non-overlapped DMRS systems. This work addresses the spectral efficiency bottleneck in MIMO-OFDM systems by enabling data transmission over the entire time-frequency grid, which is crucial for future 6G networks. The combination of analytical optimization and AI-based receiver design offers a practical path to improve performance without additional bandwidth. The analytical framework characterizes the iterative behavior between channel estimation (CE) and MIMO detection (MD) mean-square errors (MSEs) within an iterative CE and detection (ICED) process. The AI receiver incorporates Transformer encoders and an ICED structure, and simulations demonstrate spectral efficiency gains over conventional systems.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: In wireless communication, demodulation reference signals (DMRS) are used for channel estimation, but they occupy resources that could otherwise carry data. Superimposed DMRS (SI-DMRS) transmits data and pilots simultaneously, improving spectral efficiency but complicating channel estimation and detection. Iterative channel estimation and detection (ICED) processes jointly refine these tasks, and Transformer-based AI receivers can learn complex patterns to enhance performance.

## [Longitudinal Study of 33k PRs Reveals AI Collaboration Trends in vLLM and SGLang](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13884) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A longitudinal analysis of 33,228 merged pull requests from vLLM and SGLang found that development velocity increased 21x and 17.9x respectively, with bot-authored PRs contributing less than 0.2% of this growth. The study also observed a 4.2x and 3.8x increase in PR comment density, with bot comments accounting for an estimated 15-20% of that increase. This study provides empirical evidence that AI-assisted development is associated with higher throughput and broader contributor participation in high-velocity open-source projects, which has implications for biomedical AI agents and bioinformatics pipeline development. Understanding these trends can help teams optimize their workflows and anticipate the impact of AI tools on software engineering. The study analyzed seven engineering metrics across four development eras, covering vLLM (Feb 2023–Jun 2026; 18,290 PRs) and SGLang (Jan 2024–Jun 2026; 14,938 PRs). In the latest era, median cycle time was 1.04 days for vLLM and 0.62 days for SGLang, while P90 cycle times reached 16.8 and 14.3 days respectively; PR size remained relatively stable.

The findings are relevant to fields like biomedical AI agents and bioinformatics pipelines, where efficient software development is critical.

## [CipherSight: Robust HTTPS Website Fingerprinting via TLS Records](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13905) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CipherSight introduces a TLS-record-based hierarchical framework for HTTPS website fingerprinting, achieving 95.41% accuracy across over 2,000 website classes in closed-world settings and maintaining over 90% accuracy under temporal and geographic drift. It jointly encodes multiple record-level attributes and uses masked record modeling with record-resource annotations as privileged supervision. This work addresses the critical out-of-distribution and open-world challenges in website fingerprinting, which are often overlooked but essential for real-world deployment. By leveraging TLS records instead of raw TCP packets, CipherSight offers a more stable and generalizable approach, potentially improving privacy attacks and defenses in encrypted traffic analysis. The framework captures both intra-flow dependencies among TLS records and inter-flow interactions across concurrent flows, exploiting structural patterns in HTTPS traffic. It employs a masked record modeling (MRM) task and structure-aware objectives with semantic distillation to learn robust representations, outperforming all evaluated baselines under distribution shifts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Website fingerprinting (WF) aims to identify visited websites from encrypted traffic metadata, such as packet sizes and timing. Traditional methods rely on raw TCP packet sequences, which are sensitive to transport-layer artifacts and degrade under out-of-distribution (OOD) conditions caused by temporal or geographic changes. Open-world scenarios, where unseen websites are common, further challenge existing classifiers. TLS records provide a more stable representation of application-layer traffic, potentially improving robustness.

## [CForce: Consistency Forcing Improves Parallel Decoding in Diffusion LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13925) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CForce is a novel distillation method that forces early-stage mask predictions in diffusion large language models (dLLMs) to align with later-stage predictions, improving the reliability of parallel decoding. It introduces Confidence Adaptive KL Divergence as a distillation objective and provides theoretical analysis showing it approximately minimizes early-stage prediction errors. This work addresses a critical limitation of diffusion LLMs—error propagation during aggressive parallel decoding—which is essential for making these models practical for efficient language generation. By improving speed-quality trade-offs, CForce could accelerate the adoption of dLLMs in real-world applications where low latency is crucial. CForce trains the model on pre-collected self-rollout trajectories, improving training-inference alignment. The method applies to both mask-to-token decoding and edit-capable decoding, where later token-to-token refinements provide additional supervision for earlier masked-state predictions. Experiments on non-edit and edit-capable LLaDA models show improved speed-quality trade-offs, especially under high-parallelism decoding budgets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion large language models (dLLMs) generate text by iteratively denoising masked tokens, allowing parallel decoding in a single forward pass. However, aggressive parallelism can lead to unreliable early-stage predictions, causing errors that propagate. LLaDA is a prominent example of a dLLM trained from scratch, rivaling autoregressive models like LLaMA3 8B. Distillation methods like CForce aim to align training and inference to mitigate such issues.

## [EchoRec: Multi-Token Prediction with Cycle-Consistent Preference Alignment for Generative Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14011) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EchoRec introduces a novel framework that enhances generative recommendation by leveraging multi-token prediction (MTP) with cycle-consistent holistic preference alignment across multiple horizons. It comprises two modules: Horizon-aware Preference Generation (HPG) and Verifiable Holistic-Preference Alignment (VHA), which together improve recommendation accuracy and enable multi-item generation. This work addresses a gap in existing generative recommendation methods by unlocking the potential of MTP as dense supervision, which could lead to more accurate and efficient recommendation systems. The approach is theoretically grounded and shows superior performance on three datasets, making it a significant contribution to the field of recommendation systems and generative models. The HPG module sequentially chains lightweight auxiliary branches on the base recommender, each conditioning on its predecessor to respect preference evolution. The VHA module consolidates these into a holistic preference and echoes it back through cycle-consistent projectors, with theoretical guarantees excluding rank-collapse spurious alignment under an invertible transport. All auxiliary components are discarded at inference, introducing negligible overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Generative recommendation autoregressively generates semantic IDs of target items, unifying preference modeling and index retrieval in a shared token space. Multi-token prediction (MTP) trains models to predict several future tokens at once, providing richer training signals and faster generation. Semantic IDs are discrete codes representing items, often derived from embeddings, and are central to generative recommendation systems.

## [MedClaw: Heuristic Agent Harness for Long-Horizon Surgical Video Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14015) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MedClaw introduces a heuristic agent harness that separates reasoning from perception, using a text-only orchestrator and frozen vision-language sub-agents to improve long-horizon temporal reasoning in surgical videos. It also proposes a gradient-free, reward-gated Heuristic Skill Distillation loop and a new benchmark, MedClawBench, with 1,123 doctor-grounded questions. This work addresses a challenging and underexplored problem in surgical video understanding, where existing one-shot VLMs and video agents fall short. By separating reasoning from perception and adapting from only about 100 labeled examples, it offers a data-efficient and transferable approach that could improve AI-assisted surgical education and decision support. The agent harness uses a text-only orchestrator that plans evidence gathering and issues auditable tool calls, while frozen vision-language sub-agents execute each call over pixels. The Heuristic Skill Distillation loop mines low-scoring traces and keeps candidate skills only if they raise validation reward, yielding reusable retrieval skills like directed re-look. MedClawBench includes de-leaked, doctor-grounded questions over self-built long neurosurgery recordings and a held-out public lecture-video test split.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: An agent harness is the software infrastructure surrounding a large language model that enables it to operate as an AI agent, managing tool use, memory, and feedback loops. Long-horizon temporal reasoning in video understanding requires answering questions about events before, after, or across stages, which is challenging for one-shot VLMs that compress entire procedures into a context window. Video agents that train the model where to look are data-hungry and transfer poorly to out-of-domain surgery, motivating MedClaw's approach of evolving context rather than optimizing weights.

## [VLM-Based System Generates Esports Commentary for Any Gameplay Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14016) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a content-based video narration system that generates spoken, esports-style commentary for arbitrary gameplay recordings using a general-purpose vision-language model (VLM) and text-to-speech, without game-specific instrumentation or task-specific training. It employs three novel mechanisms: temporal mosaic packing, context-conditioned prompting, and duration-conditioned generation with elastic alignment. This work addresses the scarcity of live game commentary, which currently exists mainly for professional esports broadcasts. By enabling automated commentary for any gameplay video, it could democratize content creation for gamers, streamers, and media producers, potentially transforming how gameplay content is narrated and consumed. The system uses temporal mosaic packing to arrange nine uniformly sampled frames into a single 3x3 image, reducing image payloads by 9x per minute. It also supports either cloud TTS or a 6-bit quantized 4B-parameter on-device TTS model on Apple silicon, enabling fully local speech synthesis. The paper reports a qualitative case study on real-time strategy footage and candidly discusses failure modes such as hallucinated game state and prosody artifacts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI models that can process both visual and textual information, enabling tasks like image captioning and visual question answering. Text-to-speech (TTS) systems convert text into spoken audio. Traditional game commentary relies on human expertise and game-specific data, but this system aims to generalize using only visual input, making it applicable to any game without modification.

## [ForgeWM: Progressive Causal Training for Few-Step Action-Conditioned Video World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14022) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ForgeWM introduces a progressive training framework that converts a bidirectional action-conditioned video generator into efficient few-step world models, operating at steady-state denoising budgets of 1, 2, and 4 steps. It achieves state-of-the-art results on paired Minecraft trajectories and transfers to gamepad-controlled FPS gameplay. This work addresses the critical need for low-latency, action-conditioned video world models in interactive AI applications such as games and robotics. By enabling few-step synthesis with high fidelity, ForgeWM could make real-time interactive simulation more practical and accessible. The framework comprises four stages: domain adaptation, teacher-forced causal training, causal consistency distillation, and on-policy distribution matching with a bidirectional teacher. It also supports a dual-path deployment protocol with replay-time refinement, where a one-step student can re-noise and refine its saved draft, achieving quality close to four-step reference while being three times closer to the experienced trajectory than regeneration from noise.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Action-conditioned video world models are generative systems that predict future video frames conditioned on historical actions, enabling interactive applications like game AI and robot planning. Traditional video diffusion models require many denoising steps, making real-time inference challenging. Causal distillation techniques, such as those used in Causal rCM, compress the inference process to few steps, but extending them to interactive world models with discrete and continuous action inputs remains difficult. ForgeWM builds on these concepts to achieve efficient few-step synthesis while maintaining alignment with user controls.

## [LLM-based APR: Bug Complexity, Fault Localization, and Cost-efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14065) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study empirically evaluates LLM-based automated program repair (APR) across bug complexity, fault localization strategies, and cost-efficiency, using two APR techniques (ChatRepair and CodeCorrector) and three LLMs (DeepSeek, GPT, and Llama). It reveals that structurally complex bugs and imprecise fault localization significantly impact repair performance, and that higher-cost LLMs do not always yield better cost-efficiency. This research provides statistically grounded insights into underexplored aspects of LLM-based APR, guiding future tool development and research. It highlights the trade-off between repair effectiveness and computational cost, which is crucial for practical adoption of APR in software engineering. The study found that over 50% of moderately complex bugs can be repaired by low-cost LLM-based APR techniques. GPT-5 repaired 7 and 39 more complex bugs than DeepSeek-V4-pro and DeepSeek-V3.2, respectively, while DeepSeek-V3.2 showed the best cost-efficiency overall.

## [MACS: Hybrid Multi-Agent Framework for Reliable E-Commerce Recommendations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14068) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MACS (Multi-Agent Commerce System) is a new hybrid multi-agent framework for conversational recommendation in fixed-catalog e-commerce settings. It combines LLMs for language tasks with deterministic operations for correctness-critical functions, achieving a 87.1% pass rate on a 140-query benchmark and perfect brand compliance. This framework addresses the critical need for reliability in LLM-based e-commerce recommendations, where outputs must be constrained to a merchant's fixed catalog. By separating language tasks from deterministic constraint enforcement, MACS demonstrates a promising architecture for production-grade conversational recommender systems, potentially improving user trust and adoption. MACS uses a shopping agent for natural-language understanding and response generation, and a merchant agent for deterministic product retrieval, hard-constraint filtering, brand exclusion, and progressive relaxation. A session-persistent preference layer tracks constraints across turns, enabling consistent handling of budget overwrites and exclusion reversals. On a 10-scenario multi-turn benchmark, MACS achieves a macro Pass@5 of 72%, outperforming GPT+Catalog (56%) and Gemini+Catalog (52%) with zero constraint drift.

In fixed-catalog e-commerce settings, recommendations must be drawn only from a merchant's existing inventory, without web search or unsupported claims. LLMs are powerful for language understanding but can hallucinate or violate constraints, so hybrid architectures that combine LLM flexibility with deterministic rule enforcement are gaining attention for reliability-critical applications.

## [P2Skill: Privacy-Preserving Skill Distillation for Cloud-Local LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14094) ⭐️ 7.0/10

P2Skill introduces a prompt-based skill distillation method that enables a local small language model (SLM) to decompose, route, paraphrase, and reconstruct requests without fine-tuning, preserving privacy in cloud-local LLM inference. It achieves 1.69x and 3.66x higher privacy-preserved inference quality than previous baselines on a four-domain benchmark. This approach addresses a critical challenge in hybrid cloud-local LLM systems: protecting sensitive user data while leveraging cloud models' reasoning power. By avoiding fine-tuning and learned detectors, P2Skill offers a lightweight, adaptable solution that could enhance privacy in real-world applications without significant computational overhead. P2Skill uses skill prompts that are iteratively refined by a cloud LLM from execution failures, allowing the local SLM to generalize beyond memorized PII patterns. The method requires no privacy-specific fine-tuning or auxiliary detectors, and its effectiveness is demonstrated on a four-domain benchmark with significant quality improvements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Cloud-local LLM inference systems combine a large cloud model with a local small model to balance reasoning capability and data privacy. Existing privacy-preserving methods like prompt perturbation, entity masking, or fine-tuning often distort semantics or require extra training. P2Skill leverages prompt-based skill distillation, where the local model follows structured prompts to handle sensitive data locally, and the cloud model refines these prompts over time.

## [Rewriting OWL EL Ontologies into SHACL Constraints for Efficient Validation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14104) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a method to rewrite OWL EL- ontology axioms into SHACL constraints, allowing standard SHACL validators to handle implicit knowledge without separate reasoning steps. The approach is evaluated and shown to be generally more efficient in finding violations compared to the sequential approach of reasoning followed by validation. This work addresses a significant practical problem in semantic web technologies by integrating OWL reasoning into SHACL validation, potentially improving efficiency and correctness. It simplifies the validation pipeline and makes it more accessible to users who rely on standard SHACL validators. The rewriting targets OWL EL-, a fragment of OWL EL that restricts existential restrictions, and produces SHACL constraints that can be evaluated by any validator supporting SHACL core. The implementation is benchmarked against both sequential reasoning and validators with built-in reasoning support, showing better efficiency in most cases.

OWL EL is a profile of OWL 2 designed for efficient reasoning, and existential restrictions are a key feature that the rewriting method handles.

## [BGA Framework Extracts Malicious Signatures from Encrypted Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14126) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BGA, a noise-immune neural distillation framework that combines ANOVA, WGAN-GP, BiLSTM, and adaptive gated attention to extract malicious signatures from high-entropy TLS 1.3 flows, improving detection recall of rare attacks by 43.2%. This framework addresses critical challenges in encrypted traffic analysis, such as attention dilution and class imbalance, potentially improving detection of rare attacks in industrial edge environments. Its low latency suggests feasibility for real-time deployment on heterogeneous gateways, which is significant for network security. The framework uses ANOVA to separate control-plane features from cryptographic noise, WGAN-GP to synthesize minority samples, and an adaptive gated multi-head attention mechanism to suppress encryption artifacts. It achieves over 95.2% performance on CIC-IDS-2018 and Edge-IIoT benchmarks, with an inference latency of 0.2820 ms.

BGA combines statistical methods, generative models, and attention mechanisms to address these issues, aiming for real-time detection on edge devices.

## [AlignFace: Human-Aligned Face Similarity Metric with Interpretable Concept Relations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14130) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AlignFace introduces a novel face similarity metric that integrates cognitive psychology principles, including featural and configural attributes, nonlinear psychophysical response scaling, and own-group biases, to better align with human perception. It also introduces the FACETS dataset and employs visual-language modeling, gated cross-attention, concept bottleneck modeling, and neural generalized additive models for interpretable, human-aligned evaluation. This work addresses limitations of current representation-based metrics that assume a universal observer, improving alignment with diverse human subpopulations. It has significant implications for evaluating generative models in face editing and privacy protection, enabling more transparent and accurate perceptual metrics. AlignFace uses ante-hoc modeling to encode cognitive principles, with gated cross-attention for attribute-specific difference representations and concept bottleneck modeling to constrain reasoning via interpretable face attributes. Experiments show significant improvement over baseline metrics, including recent domain-free learned perceptual metrics.

## [SGSAN: Structure-Guided Spatiotemporal Attention GNN for Traffic Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14177) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SGSAN, a structure-guided spatiotemporal attention graph neural network that explicitly learns a static Directed Dependency Graph (DDG) to capture invariant macroscopic traffic propagation paths, and uses an InfoNCE-based soft-coupling mechanism to align dynamic attention with this structural prior. It also proposes a decoupled two-stage optimization framework to balance structural discovery and predictive error minimization. This work addresses the interpretability gap in deep spatiotemporal models for traffic flow prediction, which is critical for deployment in safety-critical urban systems. By providing built-in interpretability that aligns with physical traffic logic, SGSAN could enhance trust and facilitate adoption of AI in traffic management, while also contributing to the broader field of interpretable AI. SGSAN learns a static Directed Dependency Graph (DDG) to identify invariant macroscopic propagation paths, and uses an InfoNCE-based soft-coupling mechanism to anchor dynamic spatiotemporal attention to this structural prior. The model employs a decoupled two-stage optimization framework to resolve the conflict between structural discovery and predictive error minimization, and experiments on multiple real-world datasets show state-of-the-art predictive accuracy with built-in interpretability.

SimpleOPD introduces a tokenizer-agnostic on-policy distillation method that transfers long-context reasoning capabilities from the teacher model SU-01 to short-context student models. It addresses tokenizer mismatch, distribution mismatch, response length explosion, and training instability, achieving significant gains on math and science benchmarks. This work tackles a practical challenge in LLM distillation, enabling smaller models to inherit advanced reasoning without requiring tokenizer alignment. It demonstrates consistent improvements across multiple model families, potentially reducing the cost and complexity of deploying capable reasoning models. The method performs distillation in a shared text space and aligns tokens occupying identical text spans. It introduces a student reference KL loss and masks advantages of special termination tokens to stabilize training and control response length. Experiments on Qwen3, Qwen3.5, Intern-S2, GLM-4.7, and Gemma-4 show consistent gains, with Intern-S2-Preview improving by 21.2 points on ProofBench.

Long-context reasoning models can handle extensive input, but distilling them into short-context students is challenging due to tokenizer differences and distribution shifts. The student reference KL loss helps keep the student close to its initial policy, preventing drastic drift and ensuring stable length growth.

## [Color Bias in Vision Language Models: Stealth Visual Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14286) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Stealth Visual Prompts, a method that subtly alters text color and contrast in images to reveal biases in vision language models (VLMs). Experiments show that coloring positive words green shifts sentiment predictions positively, and reducing text-background contrast increases incorrect Visual Question Answering outputs. This research highlights a new form of bias in multimodal AI that can affect industrial applications like recruitment and recommendation systems. Understanding these visual styling biases is crucial for developing fairer and more reliable VLMs. The study analyzes how color variations affect the latent representations of the vision encoder, correlating with prediction shifts. The project page provides code and additional resources at https://github.com/KohsukeIde/color-bias-vlm.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Vision language models (VLMs) combine a vision encoder with a large language model to process visual and textual information. They are increasingly used in decision-making systems, but their biases are not fully understood. This work focuses on biases introduced by visual styling of rendered text, which can diverge from human semantic understanding.

## [Study Reveals Why Delay Detection in Business Processes Is Hard](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14367) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper analyzes the intrinsic difficulty of detecting delays in business processes, showing that remaining times are typically right-skewed and that aggregate metrics obscure performance on critical high-delay cases. The study, based on 14 event logs, finds that existing models capture the mode well but perform poorly on high-delay cases. This work addresses a significant gap in predictive process monitoring by highlighting that aggregate metrics can mislead model evaluation, especially for operationally critical delayed cases. It suggests that uncertainty-aware modeling is a promising direction, which could improve intervention strategies and service level compliance in business process management. The paper uncovers pronounced heteroscedasticity, showing that predictive uncertainty increases with delay magnitude. Mitigation approaches for imbalance yield limited benefits, suggesting the core problem is higher uncertainty rather than imbalance, and this correlation can be exploited to improve delay identification.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Predictive process monitoring (PPM) uses historical event logs to predict the remaining time of ongoing business process cases, enabling timely interventions to avoid missed deadlines. Remaining time prediction has advanced with deep learning, but performance is typically assessed with aggregate metrics, which may not reflect performance on rare but critical high-delay cases. A right-skewed distribution means most cases have short remaining times, while a few have very long ones, making it challenging to predict the tail accurately.

## [Hybrid LLM Framework Automates Security Annotation Generation for BPMN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14370) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new hybrid framework combines LLM-based semantic extraction with rule-based normalization and deterministic validation to automatically generate SecBPMN2 security annotations from BPMN models and natural-language requirements. In evaluations on 27 process models, it achieved higher precision (0.58 vs. 0.29) than human analysts while maintaining comparable recall (0.52 vs. 0.50). This work addresses a critical bottleneck in secure business process modeling, where deriving security annotations from natural-language specifications is manual, expert-intensive, and error-prone. By automating this process with high accuracy and speed, it enables scalable security-by-design in BPMN, potentially improving consistency and reducing modeling effort across industries. The framework uses schema-constrained mapping to ensure annotations adhere to the SecBPMN2 specification, and it reduces erroneous or misplaced annotations by nearly 50% compared to manual annotation. Annotation generation is also significantly faster than manual methods, making it practical for real-world use.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: BPMN (Business Process Model and Notation) is a standard for modeling business processes, and SecBPMN2 is an extension that adds security annotations to BPMN diagrams. These annotations represent security aspects such as confidentiality and integrity, and they are typically added manually by security analysts, which is time-consuming and prone to errors. The proposed hybrid framework leverages LLMs for semantic understanding and rule-based methods for validation, aiming to automate this task.

## [DeaMoE: Efficient MoE Architecture for Fast Small-Batch Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14385) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DeaMoE is a new Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architecture that groups experts into departments sharing most parameters, with a customized two-stage routing strategy to reduce memory-bound bottlenecks during small-batch decoding. It reduces per-step loaded weights by up to 50.9% and achieves up to 1.33x end-to-end TPOT speedup on a 7B model, and up to 2.00x and 1.97x peak speedup for DeepSeek-V3 on A40 and H100 in microbenchmarks. This addresses a critical performance bottleneck in MoE inference for real-time applications that rely on small-batch decoding, such as coding assistants and interactive systems. By reducing memory-bound overhead without significant accuracy loss, DeaMoE could enable faster and more efficient deployment of large MoE models in latency-sensitive scenarios. The architecture shares most parameters among experts in the same department, with each expert retaining a few private parameters to preserve uniqueness. The two-stage routing strategy avoids redundant loading, and the paper reports results on pre-trained 7B models and DeepSeek-V3, with microbenchmarks on A40 and H100 GPUs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) is an AI model architecture that uses multiple specialized submodels, or 'experts,' to handle tasks more efficiently than a single monolithic model. In small-batch decoding, MoE inference becomes memory-bound because loading expert weights dominates, and existing solutions like weight compression or fine-grained expert design often degrade accuracy or add overhead. DeaMoE tackles this by sharing parameters among related experts, reducing the memory footprint during decoding.

## [GBU-Palm: Multimodal Video Dataset for Palm PAD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14389) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GBU-Palm introduces a large-scale multimodal video dataset and benchmark for palm presentation attack detection, containing 21,326 videos from 105 subjects and 210 palms across six acquisition environments, including bona fide, Print, and Replay presentations, with 6,310 synchronized RGB-NIR samples. The benchmark evaluates four video architectures under environment-matched and held-out-environment settings, revealing architecture-dependent degradation under environmental shift and that RGB-NIR fusion does not consistently outperform RGB-only input. This dataset addresses a critical gap in palm presentation attack detection by providing a large-scale, multimodal, and diverse benchmark that enables systematic evaluation across environments, modalities, and attack types. It offers valuable insights into model robustness and modality fusion, which is significant for biometrics and security research, and could drive the development of more robust PAD methods. The dataset includes leakage-controlled protocols that separate palm identity and attack lineage, and the benchmark analyzes model behavior through true accept, true reject, false accept, and false reject decomposition, spectral masking, temporal-order intervention, and frozen-backbone NIR probing. These analyses reveal distinct failure patterns and evidence utilization across architectures, providing a unified and challenging benchmark for cross-environment conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Palm presentation attack detection (PAD) aims to identify spoofing attempts using printed or replayed palm images. Existing datasets are often limited by static imagery, restricted acquisition conditions, or insufficient multimodal video data, hindering systematic evaluation. GBU-Palm addresses this by providing a large-scale multimodal video dataset with synchronized RGB-NIR samples, enabling more comprehensive benchmarking. The benchmark evaluates video architectures under different environmental settings, highlighting the challenges of cross-environment generalization.

## [AER Framework Enables AI-Assisted Art Style Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14405) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the Analyze-Experiment-Resituate (AER) framework, derived from interviews with 10 professional digital artists, to support AI-assisted art style exploration. The framework was implemented in a prototype and evaluated with 16 artists in a controlled study and a two-week field study with four artists. This framework addresses a critical gap in generative AI tools, which often prioritize style replication over exploration, potentially limiting artistic creativity. By providing a structured approach that enhances artist agency and reflection, AER could influence the design of future creative tools and support professional artists in developing unique styles. The AER framework consists of three practices: analyzing artworks into interpretable stylistic elements, having controllable experimentation guided by the artist's choices, and resituating emerging styles through simulated social perspectives. The controlled study showed that AER increased artists' agency and reflection compared to a direct style-transfer workflow.

## [Bilevel NAS Framework with Neuron Gating and Mixed Activation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14443) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a general bilevel optimization framework for Neural Architecture Search (NAS) that uses continuous relaxations for neuron gating and mixed activation, enabling differentiable optimization over discrete architecture spaces. It proposes three methods—NAS-NG, NAS-MA, and NAS-NGMA—and demonstrates their effectiveness on MLPs and CNNs using MNIST and CIFAR-10. This work addresses the high computational cost and scalability challenges of NAS by replacing discrete decisions with continuous relaxations, offering a scalable alternative to discrete NAS. It could impact NAS research and practical deployment by enabling efficient discovery of compact architectures across various network types. The proposed methods consistently outperform vanilla DARTS on CIFAR-10. On MNIST, NAS-NGMA achieves 98.68% test accuracy with 7.69M MLP parameters, while NAS-NG achieves 99.63% accuracy with only 0.26M CNN parameters, and NAS-NG can also optimize over-parameterized architectures to improve accuracy while reducing parameters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Neural Architecture Search (NAS) is a subfield of hyperparameter optimization that automates the design of neural network architectures. It is naturally formulated as a bilevel optimization problem, where the upper level optimizes architecture parameters using validation performance, and the lower level trains network weights using training loss. Traditional NAS methods are computationally expensive due to discrete decisions and large search spaces; this paper proposes continuous relaxations to make the search differentiable and more efficient.

## [LP-NAS: Linear Programming-Based Neural Architecture Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14472) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LP-NAS introduces a linear programming-based framework for differentiable neural architecture search, formulating a linear program using the validation-loss gradient and training-loss Hessian to compute architecture update directions. It includes two variants, S-LP-NAS and R-LP-NAS, and demonstrates faster convergence and higher validation performance than standard DARTS on CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100. This work addresses a known limitation of differentiable NAS by providing a mathematically principled optimization approach, potentially improving generalization and efficiency in architecture search. It could influence future NAS research and make automated architecture design more accessible for practitioners. LP-NAS formulates a linear program using the validation-loss gradient and training-loss Hessian to compute an architecture update direction that improves generalization while preserving model parameter optimality. The paper introduces two computationally efficient variants, S-LP-NAS and R-LP-NAS, and applies them to the DARTS search space, resulting in S-LP-DARTS and R-LP-DARTS, which outperform standard DARTS and several variants (P-DARTS, PC-DARTS, STO-DARTS) on CIFAR-10, with transferability validated on ImageNet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Neural Architecture Search (NAS) automates the design of neural network architectures, reducing reliance on human expertise. Differentiable NAS relaxes the discrete search space into a continuous domain, enabling the use of continuous optimization techniques. LP-NAS leverages linear programming, a classical optimization method, to compute architecture updates that balance generalization and optimality, addressing limitations of gradient-based approaches like DARTS.

## [Two-Stage Diffusion Transformer Framework for Cross-Tabular Data Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14496) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a two-stage framework for cross-tabular data generation (CTDG) using diffusion transformers, which can generate synthetic data from multiple heterogeneous tables. The first stage converts each raw table into a standardized statistical table, and the second stage trains a diffusion transformer to generate synthetic statistical tables, which are then reconstructed into raw tables. This work addresses a significant gap in synthetic tabular data generation, as existing methods are mostly limited to single-table scenarios. By enabling generation from multiple heterogeneous tables, it can improve data privacy and augment datasets in fields like healthcare, where data is often distributed across multiple tables. The framework uses multivariate Gaussian sampling and inverse probability integral transform to reconstruct synthetic raw tables from generated statistical tables. Experimental results show high fidelity in statistical representations and a favorable fidelity-diversity trade-off, though the paper is a preprint and not yet peer-reviewed.

This paper builds on diffusion transformers (DiTs) and statistical table representations to address this challenge.

## [Learning-to-Transition Framework for High-Order MIMO Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14511) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a learning-to-transition (L2T) framework for high-order MIMO detection, which uses a channel-coupled Transformer and a residual-to-BER curriculum to improve both hard and soft output detection. The framework also employs a tied-to-untied transfer for soft-output iterative detection and decoding. This work addresses the challenge of efficient search over large discrete symbol spaces in high-order MIMO systems, which is critical for 5G/6G wireless communications. By integrating Transformer-based policy updates with blockwise autoregressive factorization, it offers a novel approach that could improve detection accuracy and reliability in practical systems. The L2T framework uses a channel-coupled Transformer to update both instance embedding and sampling policy at each transition, and a blockwise autoregressive factorization to capture inter-stream dependence. For soft-output reception, the hard policy is cloned into every layer of an untied soft-input soft-output iterative detection and decoding (IDD) receiver, and a multi-stage training strategy stabilizes the hard-to-soft transfer.

**Background**: MIMO detection involves recovering transmitted symbols from received signals in multiple-input multiple-output wireless systems. High-order MIMO requires efficient search over a large discrete symbol space while producing reliable soft information for channel decoding. Traditional methods like maximum likelihood detection are computationally expensive, so machine learning approaches, including Transformers, are being explored to improve efficiency and accuracy.

## [BUZZY: Training-Free Decoding to Counter Text Bias in Multimodal MCQA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28026) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BUZZY, a training-free decoding method that corrects text-induced bias in multimodal multiple-choice question answering (MCQA) by subtracting the text-only distribution from multimodal predictions. Experiments with five VLMs on five benchmarks show BUZZY achieves the highest average accuracy while reducing inference latency by over 28% compared to prior contrastive decoding approaches. This work addresses an underexplored bias in multimodal MCQA, where candidate choices act as textual priors that can mislead models regardless of visual content. By providing a simple, training-free correction, BUZZY offers a practical improvement for VLM evaluation and could influence future bias-mitigation strategies in multimodal reasoning. BUZZY is based on the observation that a model relies on visual evidence only when its multimodal distribution significantly diverges from its text-only distribution. The method subtracts the text-only distribution from the multimodal prediction, and experiments show it outperforms state-of-the-art methods in accuracy while being more efficient.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal multiple-choice question answering (MCQA) is a common benchmark for evaluating vision-language models (VLMs), but the inclusion of candidate choices in the input can introduce biases. Previous work focused on structural biases like choice preferences, while this paper highlights that choices act as textual priors, causing models to favor linguistically plausible options regardless of visual content. Contrastive decoding methods, such as Visual Contrastive Decoding (VCD), have been used to mitigate hallucinations in VLMs by contrasting distributions from different inputs, and BUZZY extends this idea to address text-induced bias in MCQA.

## [NEURON: Neuro-symbolic System Enhances Clinical Explainability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01189) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NEURON integrates SNOMED CT ontology-informed representations with machine learning models and a RAG-grounded LLM layer to generate natural-language explanations for acute heart failure mortality prediction. On the MIMIC-IV dataset, it improved AUC from 0.71-0.74 to 0.74-0.77 and outperformed SHAP-based explanations in human-aligned metrics (0.807 vs. 0.558). This addresses a critical barrier to clinical AI adoption—the lack of ontological grounding and narrative transparency in high-performing models. By improving both predictive reliability and interpretability, NEURON offers a scalable solution for deploying trustworthy, human-centered health applications. The system uses SNOMED CT ontology to bridge raw data and medical nomenclature, and a RAG-grounded LLM synthesizes SHAP feature attributions and patient-specific clinical notes into coherent explanations. Validation on MIMIC-IV for acute heart failure mortality prediction showed substantial gains in explanation quality metrics.

SHAP is a method for explaining model predictions by assigning feature attributions.

## [ESRT: Efficient Edge-Cloud Speech-to-Text Translation Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28642) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ESRT, a parameter- and bandwidth-efficient edge-cloud MLLM framework for speech-to-text translation (S2TT). It employs multi-task weighted curriculum learning to train ESRT-1B, ESRT-4B, and ESRT-12B models, and transmits only compressed tensors to the cloud, reducing tensor size by 5.1x (ESRT) and 10.2x (ESRT-Lite) compared to raw audio. This framework addresses critical challenges in deploying MLLMs for S2TT: on-device models face resource constraints, while cloud-based systems suffer from bandwidth bottlenecks and privacy risks. By balancing efficiency and privacy, ESRT could enable practical, real-world speech translation applications across 45 languages, benefiting edge devices and cloud services. ESRT retains a lightweight speech encoder and adapter on the device, transmitting only a compressed tensor to the cloud, which keeps raw speech on-device and avoids direct exposure. The models achieve state-of-the-art S2TT performance on the FLEURS benchmark across 45 languages (45x44 directions), and the code and models are released for reproducibility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have shown promise for speech-to-text translation, but deployment faces trade-offs: on-device models are limited by compute and memory, while cloud-based approaches require transmitting raw audio, which is bandwidth-intensive and raises privacy concerns. Curriculum learning is a training strategy that presents tasks in a meaningful order to improve learning, and multi-task curriculum learning extends this to multiple tasks, helping to mitigate catastrophic forgetting—a phenomenon where neural networks forget previously learned information when trained on new tasks. Tensor compression reduces the size of data tensors for efficient transmission, which is crucial for distributed systems.

## [SportD Benchmark Reveals VLMs' Risk-Averse Strategic Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14616) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SportD, a soccer-based benchmark with 1415 decision scenarios, showing that vision-language models (VLMs) select optimal actions only about 30% of the time, favoring safer, lower-value choices. Frontier VLMs also conflate action success likelihood with value, assigning higher value to more likely actions despite no such correlation in ground truth. This benchmark provides a rigorous, quantifiable testbed for evaluating physical strategic decision-making in VLMs, revealing systematic biases that could impact their deployment in embodied AI and real-world decision-making tasks. The findings highlight a critical limitation: VLMs are not just imperfect but systematically risk-averse, which could lead to suboptimal outcomes in applications like robotics and autonomous systems. The SportD dataset includes scenarios from professional men's and women's soccer games, where a VLM observes seconds before a decision and chooses the next action. Frontier VLMs place the highest-success-probability action among their top choices in 83-92% of cases, yet they show a correlation between likelihood and value (ρ=+0.30 to +0.52) compared to ground truth (ρ=-0.08). A single deliberation sentence steering toward risk can lift frontier models' performance toward that of real players.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are multimodal models that process both images and text, enabling them to describe scenes and, increasingly, to make decisions based on visual input. Strategic decision-making involves choosing actions that maximize long-term value, which requires accurately estimating both the likelihood of success and the value of outcomes. In soccer, actions have quantifiable values (e.g., expected goal contribution), making it an objective testbed for evaluating such abilities. The SportD benchmark builds on this by providing a controlled dataset of real-game scenarios to assess VLM decision-making.

## [EchoChange: Diffusion Language Model for Remote Sensing Disaster Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01856) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EchoChange introduces a multimodal discrete diffusion language model for remote sensing disaster change captioning, replacing autoregressive decoding with iterative masked-token denoising to reduce cascading factual errors. It outperforms baselines on the RSCC benchmark. This work addresses a key limitation of autoregressive models in change captioning, where early errors propagate. By enabling iterative refinement, it improves factual accuracy in disaster descriptions, which is critical for emergency response and damage assessment. EchoChange uses draft-aware dual-pass training, a progressive masking curriculum, and confidence-guided remasking to align training with iterative inference. Experiments on the RSCC benchmark show substantial improvements over both general-purpose and remote-sensing-specific baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Remote sensing change captioning (RSCC) generates natural language descriptions of changes between pre- and post-disaster image pairs. Traditional autoregressive models generate text left-to-right, so early misinterpretations become irreversible and amplify errors. Discrete diffusion language models, in contrast, generate by iteratively denoising masked tokens, allowing parallel and bidirectional refinement. The RSCC dataset, introduced in 2025, provides large-scale bi-temporal disaster image pairs with captions, enabling training and evaluation of such models.

## [Improving Robustness of Multimodal RLVR Against Prompt Variations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08802) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Prompt-Invariant RLVR (PIRL), a method that combines a dynamic trinary reward and a consistency regularizer based on an embedding-space adversary to improve generalization robustness of multimodal RLVR models. Under stress testing, PIRL's average accuracy drops by only ≤1%, compared to GRPO's ~3% drop. This work addresses a critical robustness issue in multimodal RLVR, which is essential for reliable deployment in high-stakes domains like medical VQA. By improving generalization to unseen prompts, PIRL enhances AI safety and reliability, potentially influencing future post-training methods. The paper identifies two issues in the standard RL objective: the binary verifier conflates format with content, and the training distribution covers only a thin slice of real-world prompts. PIRL separates format from semantics in the reward and applies policy invariance across perturbed prompts with equivalent semantics.

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a post-training method that fine-tunes language models using rewards from automatic, rule-based checkers, rather than learned reward models or human raters. Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) integrate multiple modalities like text and images, but their performance can be brittle to prompt variations, which is a known robustness challenge.

The paper introduces INSIDE, a framework that fine-tunes LLMs to generate internal dialogue grounded in Bloom's Taxonomy across cognitive, affective, and action dimensions, improving both action fidelity and reasoning alignment in student simulators. The framework baselines against different prompting frameworks, demonstrating its effectiveness.

## [SKILLER: Language-Level RL for Reusable Skills in Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10538) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SKILLER introduces a natural-language-driven reinforcement learning framework that automatically generates executor-specific skills for small language models, using a strong model as actor and critic while treating the small-model agent system as the environment. Experiments on Qwen3.5-9B and Qwen3.5-4B show absolute gains of 4.3–20.4 and 1.8–13.3 percentage points over baseline methods, respectively. This framework addresses the high inference cost of deploying skills on strong closed-source models by enabling small, consumer-grade GPU-deployable models to achieve comparable performance. It could significantly reduce deployment costs for agent harness systems like Codex and OpenClaw, making skill-based AI more accessible. SKILLER propagates all reinforcement learning signals entirely via natural language, without updating the parameters of the compact model. It outperforms three open-source and one closed-source skill generation or evolution methods, and matches strong closed-source models on single-skill tasks in SkillsBench.
