**Background**: Agent skills are standardized formats for packaging procedural knowledge and domain expertise, used in agent harness systems to constrain a language model's behavior for repeatable, high-quality task execution. However, strong closed-source models incur high inference costs, making deployment of these skills expensive. Open-source models deployable on consumer-grade GPUs offer a cost-effective alternative, but automatically generating effective skills for them remains challenging. SKILLER leverages natural-language-driven reinforcement learning to bridge this gap.

This paper introduces an automatic pipeline that uses LLMs to generate weighted graphs from MiniZinc problem instances, guiding variable selection in a structure-based local improvement (SLIM) framework. The approach is problem-agnostic and achieves a 39.6% average problem-weighted win rate against a one-shot Gurobi baseline, more than doubling the best single configuration (19.3%). This work demonstrates a novel way to leverage LLMs for automated structure extraction in combinatorial optimization, potentially reducing the need for manual feature engineering. It could lead to more efficient and generalizable solvers across diverse problem domains, impacting both academia and industry. The pipeline generates a uniform weighted graph for any MiniZinc instance, where nodes represent decision variables and edges represent constraint relationships. ## [LLM Confidence in Code Completion: Intrinsic Metrics as Proxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16131) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This research evaluates the confidence of large language models (LLMs) in code completion by measuring code perplexity across programming languages, models, and datasets, using a sample of 2254 files from 881 GitHub projects. It proposes intrinsic metrics like perplexity, entropy, and mutual information as proxies for functional correctness and hallucination risk. This work provides a simple, versatile, and universal method for evaluating code LLMs, which could influence how code completion models are assessed and selected in practice. It helps developers and researchers understand how language type, model choice, and code characteristics impact model confidence, aiding in the deployment of LLM-based code completion in real projects. The study finds that strongly-typed languages exhibit lower perplexity than dynamically typed languages, with scripting languages showing higher perplexity. Shell appears universally high in perplexity, while Java appears low; language-level rankings are largely stable across evaluation corpora under a fixed model, and code comments modestly increase perplexity without significantly affecting rankings.

**Background**: Code completion is a task where LLMs predict missing tokens in code, and its performance is typically measured using downstream metrics (e.g., functional correctness) or intrinsic metrics (e.g., perplexity). Perplexity is a measure of how well a probability model predicts a sample, with lower values indicating better confidence. Intrinsic metrics are simple and universal, making them attractive for evaluating LLMs without complex downstream evaluations. This research leverages these metrics to assess code LLM confidence across different languages and models.

## [TENET: Applying Test-Driven Development to Repository-Level Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24148) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TENET is a new agentic framework that applies Test-Driven Development (TDD) to repository-level code generation. It selects diverse tests, uses them for retrieval, and refines code based on test feedback, achieving 69.08% and 81.77% Pass@1 on RepoCod and RepoEval with Claude Sonnet 4, improving by 9.49 and 2.17 percentage points respectively. This work addresses key challenges in repository-level code generation, such as test selection, retrieval augmentation, and iterative refinement, which are crucial for AI-assisted software engineering. By integrating TDD principles, TENET could improve the reliability and efficiency of LLM-based development workflows, potentially influencing how developers interact with AI agents. TENET includes a test harness mechanism that selects a concise test suite to maximize diversity of target usage scenarios, a tailored agent toolset for efficient retrieval and debugging, and a reflection-based refinement workflow that iteratively analyzes failures and updates implementations. It also presents the first systematic study of how test suite characteristics influence LLM agent performance in TDD settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Test-Driven Development (TDD) is a software development practice where tests are written before the implementation code, serving as executable specifications. In repository-level code generation, LLMs are tasked with generating code across an entire codebase, which is more complex than single-function generation. TENET leverages TDD by using tests to guide retrieval and refinement, aligning with the broader trend of agentic software engineering where AI agents handle complex, goal-oriented tasks.

## [Two-Axis Framework and FusionDetect for Fake Image Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05740) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a two-axis generalization framework for fake image detection, covering both cross-generator and cross-visual-domain generalization. It also presents the OmniGen benchmark with 12 state-of-the-art generators and the FusionDetect method that combines CLIP and DINOv2 features. This work addresses a critical gap in fake image detection by emphasizing generalization across visual domains, not just across generators. The new benchmark and method could lead to more robust detectors that perform better in real-world scenarios, benefiting the AI community and applications like digital forensics. FusionDetect achieves a 3.87% accuracy improvement over the closest competitor and 6.13% higher precision on established benchmarks, with a 4.48% accuracy increase on OmniGen. The method uses frozen CLIP and DINOv2 models to create a cohesive feature space that adapts to changes in content and generator design.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Fake image detection is crucial as generative models become more advanced. Traditional detectors often struggle to generalize to unseen generators or visual domains. The OmniGen benchmark addresses this by including diverse generators, and FusionDetect leverages complementary features from CLIP and DINOv2 to improve robustness.

## [INFORM-CT: Agentic LLM/VLM Framework Automates Incidental Findings in Abdominal CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.14732) ⭐️ 7.0/10

INFORM-CT introduces a plan-and-execute agentic framework that integrates large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs) to automate the detection, classification, and reporting of incidental findings in abdominal CT scans. The framework outperforms existing pure VLM-based approaches in accuracy and efficiency on a benchmark for three organs. This work addresses a real clinical bottleneck—manual inspection of CT scans for incidental findings is time-consuming and variable. By automating this process, INFORM-CT could significantly improve radiology workflow efficiency and consistency, potentially leading to better patient outcomes and reduced radiologist workload. The framework uses an LLM-based planner that generates Python scripts from predefined base functions, and an executor that runs these scripts to perform checks and detections via VLMs, segmentation models, and image processing subroutines. Experiments were conducted in a fully automatic end-to-end manner on a CT abdominal benchmark for three organs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Incidental findings are abnormalities discovered in imaging scans that are unrelated to the original clinical indication. While often benign, they can have significant clinical implications and should be reported following established guidelines. Traditional manual inspection by radiologists is time-consuming and variable, motivating the need for automated assistance. The plan-and-execute agentic pattern is a core AI design where an agent breaks down complex tasks into structured plans and executes them step-by-step, often using tools like code generation.

## [PEFT-MuTS: Cross-Domain Fine-Tuning for Few-Shot RUL Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22631) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PEFT-MuTS, a parameter-efficient fine-tuning framework that leverages cross-domain pre-trained time-series representation models for few-shot remaining useful life (RUL) prediction. It demonstrates that knowledge transfer can work across different device types, challenging the assumption that transfer only works within similar devices. This work could significantly reduce the data requirements for RUL prediction in predictive maintenance, making it more practical for industrial applications where labeled degradation data is scarce. It also opens new avenues for cross-domain transfer learning in time-series analysis, potentially benefiting other prognostics tasks. The framework includes an independent feature tuning network and a meta-variable-based low-rank multivariate fusion mechanism to adapt univariate pre-trained backbones to multivariate degradation data. A zero-initialized regressor stabilizes fine-tuning under few-shot conditions, and experiments on aero-engine and industrial bearing datasets show effective RUL prediction with less than 1% of target equipment samples.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Remaining useful life (RUL) prediction is a key component of predictive maintenance, estimating the time until equipment failure. Traditional data-driven methods often require large amounts of degradation data from identical or similar equipment, which is often unavailable in practice. Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) is a technique that adapts large pre-trained models to new tasks by updating only a small fraction of parameters, reducing computational cost. This paper applies PEFT to time-series models, using cross-domain pre-training to enable few-shot RUL prediction.

## [AtomBridge: Inference-Time Plugin Bridges Skill-Chaining Gaps in VLA Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09430) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AtomBridge is an inference-time plugin that enables VLA models to handle long-horizon scientific tasks by inserting transitional actions between atomic skills, improving full-sequence success by 10-25% on 8-step composed tasks without fine-tuning. This addresses a critical limitation in VLA models for autonomous scientific discovery, where composed long-horizon tasks often fail due to robot-state mismatches. The plug-and-play design could accelerate the deployment of VLA-based robotic laboratories without costly retraining. AtomBridge attaches to a VLA policy fine-tuned on atomic tasks, keeping weights fixed, and uses LLM-based transition reasoning and robotic-action code generation at each task boundary. It was validated in simulation and real-world experimental environments, showing 10-25% improvement on 8-step composed tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models integrate visual understanding, language interpretation, and action execution, enabling robots to perform complex tasks. However, scientific experiments often involve long-horizon tasks composed of multiple atomic skills, and naive composition fails when the terminal state of one skill falls outside the initial-state distribution of the next, a problem known as the skill-chaining gap.

## [Systematic Comparison of Training Objectives for OOD Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.07571) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically compares four training objectives—Cross-Entropy Loss, Prototype Loss, Triplet Loss, and Average Precision (AP) Loss—for out-of-distribution (OOD) detection in image classification under standardized OpenOOD protocols. The study finds that Cross-Entropy Loss provides the most consistent near- and far-OOD AUROC overall, while other objectives can be competitive in specific settings. This comparison provides valuable insights for practitioners selecting training objectives for OOD detection, a critical component in safety-sensitive applications. By standardizing evaluation protocols, it helps clarify the relative strengths of different supervision paradigms, potentially guiding more reliable model development. The evaluation is limited to ResNet-18 with OpenOOD protocols and objective-specific OOD scoring rules. Cross-Entropy Loss, Prototype Loss, and AP Loss achieve comparable in-distribution accuracy, but Cross-Entropy Loss shows the most consistent AUROC across near- and far-OOD settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Out-of-distribution (OOD) detection aims to identify inputs that fall outside a model's training distribution, which is crucial for safety-critical applications like autonomous driving and medical diagnosis. Training objectives, such as cross-entropy, prototype-based, metric-learning, and ranking-based losses, shape the learned feature space and influence OOD behavior. OpenOOD provides a standardized benchmark and evaluation protocol to ensure fair comparisons across methods. AUROC (Area Under the Receiver Operating Characteristic curve) is a common metric for OOD detection performance.

## [Diffusion Model-Based InSAR Phase Unwrapping for Large-Scale Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21378) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a novel phase unwrapping framework based on a diffusion model, designed to handle large-scale InSAR interferograms and phase discontinuities caused by complex deformation events such as earthquakes. The method demonstrates effectiveness on both synthetic and real datasets, addressing limitations of existing learning-based approaches that rely on fixed, small input sizes. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in InSAR processing, enabling more accurate deformation monitoring for large-scale and complex events like earthquakes. By scaling to real-world interferograms and handling fault-related phase jumps, it offers a practical alternative to manual unwrapping, potentially improving the reliability of geophysical interpretations and hazard assessments. The framework leverages a diffusion model architecture to recover physically consistent unwrapped phase fields even with fault-related phase jumps. It is designed to scale to large InSAR images, overcoming the fixed-input-size limitation of many neural network approaches, and was validated on both synthetic and real datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: InSAR (Interferometric Synthetic Aperture Radar) is a remote sensing technique used for topographic mapping and deformation monitoring. Phase unwrapping is a crucial step that recovers the absolute phase from wrapped interferometric measurements, but it is challenging in complex deformation scenarios where phase continuity is disrupted. Traditional algorithms often fail in such cases, and existing deep learning methods are limited by fixed input sizes. Diffusion models, a class of generative models, have recently gained prominence in image generation and are here applied to phase unwrapping.

## [TIDE: Template-Guided Iterative Framework for Proactive Multi-Problem Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04743) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TIDE is a new framework that enables AI agents to proactively discover multiple hidden problems from context, using iterative discovery and thought templates. It outperforms single-shot and parallel multi-agent baselines on task coverage, identification, and resolution across personal workspaces and software repositories. This addresses a critical gap in current agent capabilities, as agents typically only respond to explicit user requests, missing hidden problems. TIDE could significantly improve the usefulness of AI assistants in real-world applications, leading to more proactive and comprehensive support. TIDE uses two complementary mechanisms: iterative discovery, which surfaces a small batch of candidates per round while conditioning on prior findings, and thought templates, which are reusable schemas distilled from solved cases. The framework was validated on four model backbones, showing substantial gains over baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents are increasingly used as assistants over documents, tools, and code, but they typically act only on explicit user requests, leaving many important problems undiscovered. The task of discovering multiple hidden problems from context is challenging because the total number of problems is unknown in advance. TIDE addresses this by iteratively uncovering problems and using templates to guide the discovery process.

## [Deployment-Aware Benchmarking Framework for Eye-State Recognition in Driver Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08123) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a Human-Centered Benchmarking Framework (HCBF) that evaluates eye-state recognition models using multidimensional, non-compensatory criteria beyond aggregate scores. It tests six compact models on the MRL Eye dataset, revealing that only MobileNetV3-Large and ShuffleNetV2 meet latency requirements, but none pass safety screens, resulting in an empty eligible set. This work highlights the inadequacy of relying solely on aggregate accuracy for model selection in safety-critical applications like driver monitoring. It demonstrates that deployment-aware evaluation, including robustness, transfer, latency, and explanation faithfulness, can lead to different model choices and even reject all models, promoting more responsible AI deployment. The study uses subject-disjoint MRL Eye protocol, deterministic corruptions, zero-shot transfer, and out-of-fold evaluation on RT-BENE, with TensorRT FP32 inference on NVIDIA Jetson Nano and black-box RISE faithfulness. Clean Macro-F1 ranged from 0.9566 to 0.9794, but zero-shot transfer dropped to 0.2066–0.7771, and only two models met the 33.333-ms latency deadline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Driver monitoring systems rely on eye-state recognition to detect driver drowsiness or distraction, which is safety-critical. Traditional model evaluation often uses aggregate metrics like accuracy, but this ignores real-world constraints such as inference latency on embedded devices and robustness to domain shifts. The proposed framework separates multidimensional evidence from non-compensatory eligibility, meaning a model must meet all mandatory requirements to be selected.

OCOO-T introduces a minimalist flow-matching-based virtual cell model that uses a vanilla Transformer stack directly on continuous gene expression profiles, achieving state-of-the-art performance on Tahoe100M, Replogle, and PBMC benchmarks. It integrates perturbation embeddings, dosage, and cell-line/cell-type specificity via adaptive layer normalization and in-context tokens. This work challenges the trend of increasingly complex architectures in AI virtual cell modeling, showing that a simple design can achieve top results, potentially improving scalability and generalizability. It could accelerate drug discovery and gene regulatory network elucidation by enabling more efficient in-silico perturbation simulations. OCOO-T formulates perturbation response prediction as a continuous-time denoising process and uses patching/depatching of cellular contexts to handle long transcriptional profiles. The model is evaluated on three benchmarks, demonstrating effectiveness across diverse perturbations and cell types.

## [Diagnostic Protocol Reveals Event-Conditioned Physical Fields in World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28455) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a controlled diagnostic protocol to analyze how event-conditioned physical information is organized in the latent dynamics of passive object-state world models. It shows that event contexts systematically reweight kinematic, contact, and object-permanence field readouts, and that suppressing field-aligned directions can degrade event-relevant prediction. This work addresses a critical gap in interpretability by moving beyond prediction accuracy to understand how physical information is encoded and used inside world models. The findings have potential implications for robotics and physical reasoning, where understanding latent structure is key to building more reliable and controllable AI systems. The protocol tests event-regime encoding, reweighting of non-exclusive physical field readouts, and functional consequences of field-aligned components. Using a balanced controlled-generator dataset with free-motion, collision, and occlusion events, they evaluate recurrent, attention-based, and latent state-space transition models under fixed-horizon forecasting, with causal field effect (CFE) analysis showing strongest evidence for contact-aligned structure in collision-contact windows.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: World models are AI systems that maintain latent state representations and transition dynamics to predict future states. This paper focuses on passive object-state world models, which predict physical states without actions. The diagnostic protocol aims to uncover how physical information like kinematics, contact, and object permanence is organized in the latent space, going beyond mere prediction accuracy.

## [TypeProbe: Probing Code Models for Cross-Lingual Type Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08339) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces TypeProbe, a probing method that recovers type representations from the residual streams of pre-trained code models. It demonstrates that cross-lingual type representations emerge even from untyped code, and that these representations are partly robust to lexical perturbations and cross-language syntactic variations. This work advances interpretability of code models by directly targeting formal type semantics, a previously underexplored area. Understanding how code models encode types can lead to better model design, debugging, and cross-lingual code understanding, benefiting developers and researchers in software engineering and AI. The study uses a parallel dataset of Java and Python code examples, training probes on one language to infer argument and result types in the other. The findings show that hidden states linearly encode the result type implied by typed function application, and this structure is partly robust to perturbations.

**Background**: Probing is a technique used to interpret neural networks by training classifiers to predict linguistic or semantic properties from internal representations. The residual stream in transformer models is the cumulative hidden state that carries information across layers, often analyzed for interpretability. Cross-lingual type representations refer to the ability of a model to encode type information in a language-agnostic manner, which is valuable for multilingual code understanding.

## [ShortOPD: Short-to-Long On-Policy Distillation Recovers Pruned LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13124) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ShortOPD introduces a short-to-long on-policy distillation schedule that detects teacher-confirmed repetitive suffixes and allocates rollout budgets to effective lengths, recovering pruned LLMs for free-form generation. It improves compressed model scores to about 9x their unrecovered value and 1.6-4.4x over standard recovery recipes, matching a fixed 8192-token horizon within two points using a quarter of the training time. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM compression research by focusing on free-form generation rather than multiple-choice tasks, which is what deployment actually requires. The proposed method could enable more practical deployment of compressed LLMs, potentially reducing inference costs while maintaining generation quality. The method uses on-policy distillation with a frozen pre-compression teacher, and detects teacher-confirmed repetitive suffixes to determine effective rollout lengths. Experiments across math, code, and open-ended generation show significant improvements, but the paper is a preprint and not yet widely validated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Structured pruning removes entire channels, attention heads, or layers to create smaller, dense models that run efficiently on standard hardware. However, pruned models often collapse on free-form generation tasks, even though they perform well on multiple-choice benchmarks. On-policy distillation is a technique where the student model generates its own trajectories and a high-performing teacher grades each token, providing dense supervision. The pass@k metric measures the probability that at least one of k generated samples is correct, and is commonly used to evaluate code generation models.

## [New Reward Model Debiases Text-to-Image Evaluation via Cultural Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15740) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced an Implicit Cultural Alignment Reward Model built on a 4.2B-parameter multimodal large language model (MLLM), integrating an Implicit Cultural Probe and a Skip-connection Cross-Attention (SkipCA) mechanism. On the CulturalFrames benchmark, it achieved 82.12% pairwise accuracy and a 10× speedup over standard VQA-based evaluators. This addresses a critical gap in text-to-image (T2I) evaluation by tackling cultural bias, which is often overlooked by existing metrics and judges. The efficient, culturally aware reward model can improve preference optimization pipelines like RLHF and DPO, making generative AI fairer and more trustworthy across diverse cultures. The model processes each evaluation in 0.21 seconds under local inference, achieving a 10× speedup by bypassing autoregressive text generation. It also reported Pearson and Kendall correlation coefficients of 0.585 and 0.412, respectively, on 3,323 curated image pairs from the CulturalFrames benchmark.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-image (T2I) systems generate images from text prompts, and evaluating their cultural authenticity is crucial for fair and trustworthy AI. Existing evaluation metrics and multimodal judges often rely on visual-semantic representations that underrepresent implicit cultural norms, leading to biased judgments. The proposed model uses a lightweight MLLM with a SkipCA mechanism to better preserve culturally salient details, and it avoids autoregressive text generation for faster inference.

## [GTIN: Unified Framework for Joint Event and Time Prediction in Temporal Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23556) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces GTIN, a unified mathematical framework for jointly predicting the next event and its occurrence time in temporal graphs. Empirical evaluations show it consistently outperforms existing methods, especially with irregular event patterns and complex temporal dependencies. This addresses an understudied problem in dynamic network analysis, offering a robust foundation for future research. It could improve applications in social, financial, and traffic networks where anticipating both event type and timing is critical. GTIN analyzes the entire graph structure rather than relying solely on local neighborhoods, enhancing its expressiveness. The framework is flexible enough to accommodate a wide range of network structures and temporal dynamics, as demonstrated across multiple datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Temporal graphs model dynamic systems where vertices and edges change over time, capturing temporal dependencies between entities. Event prediction in such graphs typically focuses on predicting future events, but jointly predicting event type and timing is more challenging and less explored. Existing methods often treat these tasks separately or rely on local information, limiting their effectiveness.

## [Negative-Control Protocol for EEG Foundation-Model Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24519) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a negative-control protocol for EEG foundation-model benchmarks, showing that five models can decode dataset identity with perfect accuracy (1.000 AUROC) and that classical features outperform foundation models on matched clinical tasks (e.g., 0.734 vs. 0.677 macro-AUROC on CAUEEG). This work addresses a critical methodological flaw in EEG foundation-model evaluation, suggesting that reported gains may stem from dataset identity rather than clinical signal. It provides a reporting protocol to improve the reliability of future benchmarks, impacting researchers and clinicians who rely on these models. The study evaluated five models on five tasks across four benchmark datasets plus CAUEEG, using subject-disjoint validation. Key results include that all encoders decoded dataset identity at 1.000 before and after PCA-50, and a randomly initialized encoder outperformed pretrained REVE on CAUEEG (0.667 vs. 0.570).

**Background**: EEG foundation models are large-scale machine learning architectures that learn universal representations from heterogeneous EEG data via self-supervised learning. The macro-AUROC metric summarizes classification performance across classes, where 0.5 indicates random guessing and 1.0 perfect performance.

## [RepBench: Benchmark-Grounded Data Layer for LLM Capability Probing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28008) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RepBench introduces a large-scale, benchmark-grounded data layer for probing and steering LLM capabilities, compiling 13,427 papers into a taxonomy of 182 capability clusters and harvesting 46,149 audited probe texts from 353 benchmarks covering 94 capabilities. This addresses reproducibility and capability alignment in representation engineering, providing a reusable workflow that reduces dependence on single benchmarks and enables more reliable cross-model comparisons, benefiting interpretability research and model evaluation. The multi-benchmark design shows that benchmark-pooled capability vectors exhibit an interior clustering optimum on all 12 evaluated models, with low agreement to the human taxonomy. Cross-benchmark transfer evaluation reveals that difference-in-means achieves the highest model-level mean on ten models, while logistic regression wins the most capability-model cells, indicating that readout method and aggregation criterion are meaningful evaluation dimensions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Representation engineering (RepE) is a top-down approach to AI transparency that focuses on population-level representations rather than individual neurons, enabling monitoring and manipulation of high-level cognitive phenomena. Traditional evaluation of RepE methods often relies on paper-specific synthetic data, making results difficult to compare or reproduce. RepBench addresses this by grounding probing data in public benchmarks, providing a standardized resource for capability-aligned representation analysis.

## [Label-Free Criterion for UDA Algorithm Selection in Medical Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28125) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a label-free criterion that jointly selects the best UDA algorithm and hyperparameters for medical imaging, using an agreement reference built from multiple selection signals without target labels. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in deploying UDA in clinical practice, where the lack of target labels makes model selection challenging. It enables more reliable and practical UDA deployment, potentially improving diagnostic accuracy across different medical imaging domains. The method constructs a two-level agreement reference: first, multiple label-free signals nominate a model per algorithm; second, nominated models are aggregated to form reference predictions. It was validated on four brain MRI and four chest X-ray datasets across seven transfer scenarios, outperforming existing selection methods.

## [Cloud-ScPO: Mining Preference Signals from Hidden-State Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01014) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Cloud-ScPO is a new semi-supervised framework that derives preference supervision for LLM reasoning from the geometric structure of hidden states, using a small labeled set to build reference clouds and score trajectories via topology-guided soft k-NN. It consistently improves over ScPO on GSM8K and MATH-Numeric, with gains up to 4.49% and 4.19% respectively. This work reduces reliance on expensive preference labels (verified answers, human annotations, or reward models) by leveraging the model's internal representations, which could make preference optimization more scalable and accessible. It also opens a new direction of using hidden-state geometry for semi-supervised alignment in reasoning tasks. The framework represents each trajectory as a mean-pooled hidden state and scores it against connectivity-induced components using a component-level soft k-NN measure averaged across reference banks. It combines this cross-problem Cloud signal with prompt-level self-consistency: self-consistency determines answer-level preference direction, while Cloud scoring selects concrete trajectories and filters pairs by score margin.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Preference optimization (e.g., DPO) improves LLM reasoning but typically requires reliable chosen-rejected pairs, which are costly to obtain. Semi-supervised preference optimization (SSPO) aims to reduce this cost by using limited labeled data and abundant unlabeled data. This paper explores using the geometric organization of hidden states across problems as a source of preference supervision, building on prior work that analyzed hidden-state geometry for reasoning.

## [AI Agents Can Simulate A/B Tests but Need Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02345) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a validation framework called Simulated Randomized Controlled Trial (S-RCT) for using AI agents to simulate A/B test outcomes, validated on 67 historical marketing experiments. A baseline off-the-shelf foundation model captures directional signal (sign overlap 0.70) but systematically overshoots effect magnitudes, while a two-phase pre-period calibration reduces squared prediction error by ~77x and a within-subject design reduces standard errors by ~2.4x. This work addresses a practical bottleneck in the tech industry where A/B tests consume significant traffic, engineering effort, and time. If AI agents can reliably simulate outcomes, companies could vet candidate treatments before committing live traffic, accelerating experimentation and reducing costs. The framework is agent-agnostic, allowing any behavioral model from fine-tuned specialists to general-purpose foundation models to serve as the simulation engine. The two-layer error decomposition separates agent approximation error from subsampling error, enabling targeted improvements. The paper also discusses limitations and identifies applications where agentic signals are most beneficial.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: A/B testing, also known as randomized controlled experiments, is the gold standard for causal inference in tech and business, but it is resource-intensive. Simulated randomized controlled trials (S-RCTs) have been used in fields like medicine and marketing to mimic real trials using simulations, often leveraging observational data or Monte Carlo methods. This paper extends the concept by using AI agents as the simulation engine, aiming to reduce the cost and time of real experiments.

## [DialectS2S: End-to-End Speech Dialogue Model for Low-Resource Chinese Dialects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08067) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DialectS2S introduces an end-to-end speech dialogue model specifically designed for low-resource Chinese dialects, featuring a scalable data synthesis pipeline and a two-stage post-training strategy with self-aligned speech supervision. The model supports Mandarin, English, Sichuanese, Cantonese, and the Tianjin dialect, and consistently outperforms existing baselines in dialect consistency, response quality, and speech intelligibility. This work addresses a critical gap in end-to-end speech dialogue models, which have been predominantly optimized for mainstream languages and fail in low-resource dialect scenarios. By providing an efficient and scalable solution, DialectS2S enhances accessibility and inclusivity of speech technology for millions of dialect speakers, and its open-source release facilitates further research and practical applications. The model employs a two-stage post-training strategy with self-aligned speech supervision to mitigate semantic inconsistency between hidden representations and speech targets during dialect adaptation. The scalable synthesis pipeline rewrites existing dialogue data to construct dialect speech dialogue datasets, and the framework is fully open-sourced including model checkpoints, training datasets, and fine-tuning code.

**Background**: End-to-end speech dialogue models, such as SpeechGPT2, directly process and generate speech without intermediate text, enabling natural voice interactions. However, these models are typically trained on mainstream languages and struggle with low-resource dialects due to scarce data and evolving semantic representations during adaptation. Self-supervised learning has been a key technique in speech processing, allowing models to learn from unlabeled data, and self-aligned supervision extends this idea by aligning supervision signals with the model's evolving representations.

## [TdSciNER: Type-Driven Multi-Task Learning Boosts LLM-Based Scientific NER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08636) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces TdSciNER, a type-driven approach that combines an entity type filter model, an auxiliary multi-class entity typing task, and a demonstration selection strategy to enhance LLM-based scientific named entity recognition (SciNER). Experiments on three datasets show that TdSciNER achieves performance comparable to fully supervised models. This work addresses a practical limitation of LLMs in scientific text mining, where complex entity types can overwhelm the model. By filtering candidate types and enriching representations, TdSciNER improves accuracy and offers a scalable solution for information extraction across scientific domains, potentially reducing the need for extensive labeled data. TdSciNER first uses an entity type filter model to identify likely entity types in a sentence, then integrates an auxiliary multi-class entity typing task within a multi-task learning framework. It also employs a demonstration selection strategy based on sentence similarity and entity type diversity to activate in-context learning. The method is validated on three datasets, with each component contributing to performance gains.

**Background**: Named entity recognition (NER) is a subtask of information extraction that identifies and classifies entities in text. In scientific domains, entity types are more complex than in general text, and large language models (LLMs) often struggle when too many candidate types are provided in prompts. TdSciNER leverages entity type information to guide LLMs, using multi-task learning and in-context learning to improve performance.

## [MusicLayout: Explicit Structural Planning for Controllable Text-to-Music Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09035) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MusicLayout introduces an explicit intermediate representation that describes musical pieces as time-aligned layouts of sections, textures, repetitions, variations, and instrument arrangements, serving as a planning layer between text prompts and audio generation. The model first generates this MusicLayout representation and then predicts audio tokens conditioned on it within a single autoregressive sequence, enabling inspection and modification before audio synthesis. This addresses a significant limitation in current text-to-music systems, where structural organization is implicit and hard to control. By providing explicit layout-level control, MusicLayout could improve long-range structural coherence and enable users to edit musical structure before audio generation, potentially influencing future research in controllable music generation. The framework is built on a unified autoregressive formulation, where the model generates the MusicLayout representation and audio tokens in a single sequence. The authors evaluate through layout-conditioned generation, layout manipulation experiments, and matched-data ablations, and have released the implementation as open source on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-music generation has advanced rapidly, but most systems rely on global text prompts, leaving structural organization implicit. MusicLayout introduces an explicit intermediate representation that acts as a planning layer, allowing users to inspect and control musical structure before audio generation. This approach is similar to using intermediate representations in other generative domains, such as layout-to-image generation.

## [Depth-Aware Expert Masking Reveals Layer Sensitivity in MoE Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13565) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper presents a systematic layer-wise sensitivity analysis of the Qwen3.6-35B-A3B model using magnitude-based expert masking on the XLCoST benchmark. It finds that early and middle layers are fragile, while late layers tolerate aggressive masking, with a very-late policy retaining 419/500 Good+Similar outputs while masking only 640 experts. This work provides empirical grounding for depth-aware MoE compression, showing that uniform masking is suboptimal. It could guide more efficient model compression and pruning strategies for large MoE models, impacting deployment and cost reduction. The study uses Qwen3.6-35B-A3B with 40 MoE layers, 256 experts per layer, and top-8 routing, evaluated on XLCoST across 100, 300, and 500 prompt scales on three H100 servers. It also explores reducing top-k routing from 8 to 6 active experts, showing wall-clock speedup without quality loss, but not yet composing cleanly with aggressive masking.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures scale large language models while keeping computational efficiency via sparse activation. Expert masking is a technique to prune or compress models by disabling certain experts, and this paper analyzes which layers are more sensitive to such masking, providing insights for model compression.

## [SemPlan Benchmark Evaluates LLM Semantic Planning for Enterprise Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13612) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The SemPlan Benchmark paper introduces a deterministic synthetic bilingual benchmark with 1,800 cases (1,200 frozen for evaluation) in English and Brazilian Portuguese, comparing four LLM-based architectures for structured semantic planning over enterprise data. Across 4,800 primary records, answer correctness was low (22.25%–25.67%), with the semantic-request generation variant (A3) achieving the highest correctness and significantly outperforming the others. This benchmark provides a systematic evaluation framework for a critical but under-explored problem: translating underspecified natural-language requests into governed, executable actions over enterprise data. The findings highlight trade-offs between correctness, policy compliance, cost, and stability, guiding future development of more reliable LLM-based data interfaces. The four architectures are direct SQL generation (A1), a bounded tool-agent baseline (A2), structured semantic-request generation followed by deterministic planning and execution (A3), and a clarification/stateful semantic-plan variant (A4). A1 had the highest policy-correct rate and lowest unsafe-or-invalid rate, while A4 had the lowest mean API cost and lowest false-refusal rate; answer-correct repeatability on a 150-case subset ranged from 92.00% to 98.67%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Natural-language interfaces to enterprise data need to handle underspecified requests while ensuring governance, cost control, and determinism. The SemPlan benchmark addresses this by simulating realistic scenarios and comparing different architectural choices. The results indicate that adding structural constraints changes failure modes and efficiency but does not monotonically improve correctness, suggesting a trade-off rather than a universal best architecture.

## [ConceptFlow: Interactive Nested Diagrams for Formal Concept Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13630) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ConceptFlow, a new scikit-learn-compatible Python library for Formal Concept Analysis, has been released. It constructs and renders interactive nested line diagrams from many-valued formal contexts, demonstrated on Eurovision Song Contest winners from 1975 to 2025. This library provides a novel, accessible tool for visualizing complex data relationships through nested line diagrams, potentially benefiting researchers and analysts in data mining and knowledge discovery. Its application to Eurovision data showcases how FCA can reveal hidden dependencies between voting patterns and musical attributes. ConceptFlow performs conceptual scaling, computes factor lattices, and identifies filled nodes of the subdirect product to produce interactive visualizations. The Eurovision case study uses an outer scale for voting support (regional, cultural, historical, political) and an inner scale for musical characteristics (tempo and key).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Formal Concept Analysis (FCA) is a method for deriving a concept hierarchy from objects and their properties, introduced by Rudolf Wille in 1981. Nested line diagrams are a visualization technique that decomposes a concept lattice into multiple scales, making it easier to interpret complex relationships. Conceptual scaling transforms many-valued attributes into binary ones based on predefined scales.

## [Grounding Without Corrective Control: Truth-Tracking Profiles for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14252) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces 'route profiles' as a framework to analyze how large language model outputs can be answerable and truth-tracking without corrective control, using text-only models as a limiting case. This work provides a novel conceptual framework for understanding truth-tracking in LLMs, which is relevant to AI alignment and interpretability. It could help researchers better predict and improve model reliability without relying on corrective mechanisms. The paper distinguishes between 'derivative answerability' (inherited constraint from training) and 'live answerability' (current route for fresh discrepancies). It suggests that self-consistency, retrieval, tools, code execution, multimodal input, and feedback can selectively help, and that surface improvement and truth-tracking improvement can come apart.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are trained on text data and can generate coherent responses, but whether their representations truly connect to the world is debated. This paper proposes that world-connection is not binary but a profile of participation in various stabilizing routes, such as perception, testimony, coherence, and error-correction. The concept of 'corrective control' refers to the ability to detect and repair discrepancies through live, independent routes.

## [Shift-Aware Dual-Encoder Transfer Learning Improves PM2.5 Forecasting in Data-Limited Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14456) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a shift-aware dual-encoder transfer learning framework for PM2.5 forecasting, which combines a pretrained source encoder with a target-specific branch. Evaluated on two years of hourly data from 77 Taiwan stations, the best model achieved MSE = 21.6575, MAE = 3.1383, and R^2 = 0.8739, outperforming four baselines. This work addresses a critical challenge in air quality forecasting: limited target-domain data and distribution shifts between regions. By showing that source knowledge can be effectively transferred while preserving target-specific information, it offers a practical solution for deploying forecasting models in data-scarce environments, potentially improving public health monitoring worldwide. The source encoder was pretrained on hourly data from 10 U.S. monitoring locations, then adapted to Taiwan data using a chronological train-validation-test split. Ablation studies revealed that removing the Taiwan-specific branch caused the largest performance drop, and allowing the source encoder to adapt under target supervision yielded the best results. SHAP analysis identified recent PM2.5 observations and meteorological variables as key predictors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Transfer learning aims to leverage knowledge from a source domain to improve learning in a target domain, but it can suffer from negative transfer when domains differ significantly. PM2.5 forecasting is crucial for public health, yet data scarcity in many regions limits model accuracy. This study proposes a dual-encoder architecture to balance source knowledge and target-specific learning, addressing distribution shifts explicitly.

## [Proxy-Validated LLM UX Micro-Simulations: Artifact-First Protocol](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13563) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a proxy-validation protocol for LLM-driven UX micro-simulations, using embedding-based alignment metrics (top-k Jaccard and distributional weighted-Jaccard) to validate simulated friction themes against public proxy corpora like app reviews and support tweets. The authors report that embedding-based alignment outperforms lexical baselines (e.g., W=0.128 vs 0.000 on Gojek) and provide bootstrap confidence intervals revealing instability in point estimates. This work addresses the challenge of validating LLM-generated UX feedback without extensive user studies, which is crucial for early-stage teams with limited resources. By providing a lightweight validation protocol, it could enable more reliable use of LLM simulations in iterative design and decision-making, impacting HCI and LLM application research. The protocol uses two alignment metrics: top-k Jaccard and distributional weighted-Jaccard (W), and compares lexical, TF-IDF, and multilingual embedding baselines across six proxy datasets. The paper also ablates four agent strategies (single-pass, best-of-N, hybrid, and score-then-select judge) and includes a failure-mode analysis of grounding and fabrication proxies.

## [SAGE: Adaptive Surrogate Gradients for Spiking Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13702) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SAGE introduces an uncertainty-modulated surrogate-gradient mechanism for Transformer-based spiking neural networks (SNNs), using normalized self-attention entropy to adapt the surrogate-gradient slope during training. Experiments on CIFAR-10/100 show consistent accuracy improvements of 1-2% over fixed-surrogate baselines without changing the inference model. This work addresses a key challenge in SNN training—the suboptimality of fixed surrogate gradients—by leveraging attention-derived uncertainty as a lightweight training signal. It could improve the accuracy and practicality of Transformer-based SNNs, which are promising for energy-efficient AI, and may inspire further adaptive training methods in the SNN community. SAGE modulates only the training-time surrogate parameter, preserving the original architecture and deployment cost. The method estimates block-level uncertainty from normalized self-attention entropy and adapts the surrogate-gradient slope accordingly, with experiments conducted across multiple simulation time steps on CIFAR-10/100.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Spiking neural networks (SNNs) are energy-efficient due to sparse event-driven computation, but training them is challenging because the spike function is non-differentiable. Surrogate gradients provide a differentiable approximation during backpropagation, but their fixed shape may be suboptimal across layers and training stages. Self-attention entropy, derived from Transformer attention maps, can serve as a measure of uncertainty, which SAGE exploits to adapt the surrogate gradient.

## [AdsWorldEngine: Self-Evolving Conversational Advertising Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13833) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AdsWorldEngine is a novel agentic framework for conversational advertising that uses an orchestrator-tool coevolution training procedure, where the orchestrator is trained with supervised fine-tuning and agentic reinforcement learning, and tools are improved using preference data from high- and low-reward rollouts. In offline evaluations, it improves ad diversity by 60% and relevance by 80% over the current production system, and in an online A/B test, it increases RPM by 22% and ad coverage by 74%. This work addresses the challenge of delivering useful, non-intrusive ads in multi-turn AI assistant interactions, a growing area as conversational AI becomes a primary interface. The self-improving loop and significant performance gains suggest a scalable approach to commercializing AI assistants while maintaining user experience. The framework includes an Opportunity Gate to decide whether to show ads, an Orchestrator to generate commercial intents and construct a top-3 ad slate, and an Evaluator for offline optimization. It also introduces label grounded judgment modeling, which uses human labels with thinking traces, reflection to filter inconsistent rationales, and a cost-sensitive GRPO variant to optimize binary judgments while preserving asymmetric reward gaps.

**Background**: Conversational advertising aims to deliver relevant ads within multi-turn assistant interactions, unlike traditional query-based advertising where intent is explicit in a short query. This requires inferring latent commercial intent from dialogue context and deciding whether an ad is helpful or intrusive. Agentic reinforcement learning (Agentic RL) is a paradigm where LLMs are treated as learnable policies in sequential decision-making loops, enabling them to use tools and adapt based on feedback. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm used to optimize language models.

It could enable more robust and efficient federated learning in healthcare and wearable device applications, where data privacy and resource constraints are critical. The study evaluates HQKAN against MLP for five-class arrhythmia classification on MIT-BIH and three-class on INCART under FedAvg. HQKAN improves most aggregate and minority-class metrics while reducing trainable parameters by 37.35% (MIT-BIH) and 44.81% (INCART), and communication cost by 24.89% and 36.41%, respectively.

## [LLMs as Sounding Boards in Songwriting: A Case Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13944) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper presents a first-person case study of using LLMs as interpretive sounding boards in songwriting from July 2025 to March 2026, based on 16 original pieces. It identifies two failure modes—sycophantic drift and magical overinterpretation—when calibration is absent. This research shifts the focus from AI as a generator to AI as an interpretive partner, which could influence how creatives integrate AI into their workflows. It highlights both the potential and risks of using LLMs in creative practice, offering insights for human-AI collaboration. The study draws on Hartmut Rosa's theory of resonance, arguing that resonance occurs between the author and their own material, mediated by the model. The author found that sustained calibration by the user supports resonance, while its absence leads to sycophantic drift and magical overinterpretation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Hartmut Rosa's resonance theory describes the quality of our relationship to the world, emphasizing moments of genuine connection. Sycophantic drift refers to AI models aligning responses with user views even when incorrect, while magical overinterpretation involves assigning meaning to random patterns. This paper applies these concepts to creative practice, exploring a novel role for LLMs.

The paper introduces a unified framework that bridges systematic hazard analysis and runtime enforcement using hazard-informed safety envelopes, enabling safe urban mobility for heterogeneous robotic systems. This framework addresses the critical challenge of maintaining safety in complex human-robot interactions as robots deploy across diverse urban zones. It could significantly improve the reliability and trustworthiness of physical AI systems in public spaces, impacting robotics and AI safety research. The framework treats safety not as a static constraint but as a dynamic property enforced at runtime through a physical AI harness. It integrates hazard analysis with runtime enforcement, ensuring safety guarantees across different levels of abstraction.

This paper introduces data-driven surrogate models that approximate equilibrium traffic flows from origin-destination demand, enabling scalable road maintenance scheduling. The approach is validated using real-world traffic data from Newark, New Jersey. This work addresses the computational bottleneck of embedding equilibrium traffic assignment in maintenance scheduling, potentially enabling more efficient and practical infrastructure planning. It could benefit transportation agencies and researchers by making network-level maintenance optimization more tractable. The surrogate models are trained using optimization-based equilibrium solutions as ground truth, and they directly predict arc flows from origin-destination demand. The case study on Newark, NJ data demonstrates the effectiveness of the approach as a scalable building block for future maintenance scheduling frameworks.

## [Uncertainty-Aware Deep Learning Framework for Sex Attribution in Prehistoric Hand Stencils](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14539) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces an uncertainty-aware deep learning framework that explicitly models, propagates, and aggregates uncertainty for attributing biological sex to prehistoric hand stencils. The framework combines dual image processing, dual contour extraction, structured silhouette augmentation, model architectural diversity, and ensemble-based decision aggregation to generate predictions with confidence measures. This work bridges deep learning and archaeology, offering a robust and reproducible method for decoding ancient rock art, which has been challenging due to missing ground truth and image degradation. The uncertainty-aware approach could set a precedent for applying AI in other fields with high uncertainty and limited labeled data, enhancing the reliability of scientific inferences. The pipeline generates twelve plausible silhouette realizations per stencil to capture boundary uncertainties, processed by two ensembles of ten deep neural networks each (EfficientNet-B3 and MobileViT-S) trained on 14,036 contemporary hand samples. On contemporary data, ensemble models achieve accuracies exceeding 88% in older age groups, and the framework also uses a triangulated validation scheme integrating UMAP + k-NN and LayerCAM for anatomical consistency.

**Background**: Determining the sex of prehistoric hand stencil creators is difficult due to the absence of ground truth, population differences, and image degradation. Traditional morphometric methods suffer from high structural overlap across sexes and poor cross-population generalizability. Uncertainty-aware deep learning frameworks explicitly model predictive uncertainty, improving reliability in classification tasks. Ensemble methods combine multiple models to enhance performance and reduce overfitting.

## [LLMs and Few-Shot Learning for Sex-Based Blood Pressure Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01826) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This study demonstrates the feasibility of using large language models (LLMs) and few-shot learning to automatically extract blood pressure (BP) values and biological sex information from scientific literature. The authors experimented with LLaMA3 and GPT-3.5, along with a few-shot method, on a manually reviewed subset of 213 articles, and generated visualizations to analyze sex-based BP variations. This work addresses a critical gap in current BP standards, which often lack demographic adjustments for sex and age. By automating the extraction of sex-specific BP data from literature, it could enable more personalized and accurate diagnostic criteria, impacting clinical practice and public health. The study used a Solr-based search engine to retrieve articles from PubMed, and created a manually reviewed subset of 213 articles, of which 90 reported BP values by sex. The authors compared one few-shot learning method and two zero-shot LLM-based methods (LLaMA3 and GPT-3.5) for extracting means and standard deviations of BP, and generated heatmaps and contour plots to visualize sex-based differences.

## [LLM-Advisor: Cost-efficient Terrain-Aware Path Planning with LLM Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01236) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces LLM-Advisor, a framework that uses multimodal prompts and deterministic validation to refine terrain-aware path planning, along with a new benchmark MultiTerraPath containing 2,000 maps. Experiments show that LLM-Advisor with GPT-5.5 improves 52.00% of Easy maps and 45.00% of Hard maps on MultiTerraPath, while maintaining 100% validity. This work addresses a key limitation in fixed-graph path planning by leveraging LLMs to propose lower-cost routes, potentially improving efficiency in robotics and autonomous navigation. The introduction of MultiTerraPath provides a standardized benchmark for future research in terrain-aware path planning. The framework combines a multimodal prompt, map-disjoint Reference Example Augmentation (REA), and deterministic validation with fallback, ensuring that proposed routes are only deployed if feasible and strictly lower in cost. Using coarse-lattice A* with stride r=50 as baseline, LLM-Advisor recovers up to 49.16% and 29.98% of the coarse-oracle gap on Easy and Hard maps when r=100.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Terrain-aware path planning integrates terrain geometry and physical properties to generate safe and efficient trajectories for mobile robots. Traditional fixed-graph planners may miss lower-cost corridors available in native-resolution maps, and LLMs can provide semantic context to propose alternatives. The paper introduces MultiTerraPath, a controlled benchmark with Easy and Hard subsets, to evaluate such refinement methods.

## [Teffic-Audio: A General Speech Deepfake Detection System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28351) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Teffic-Audio, a general speech deepfake detection system, achieves a pooled EER of 1.454% on the 14 test sets of Speech-DF-Arena, outperforming all currently public systems on the leaderboard. It uses a Conformer-based encoder with multi-head attentive statistics pooling and a binary classifier, improving generalization through a training recipe that integrates multi-source data, attack- and source-balanced sampling, and diverse audio augmentation. This work addresses the critical need for robust generalization in speech deepfake detection across heterogeneous spoofing conditions, which is essential for practical deployment. By achieving state-of-the-art performance with a simple architecture and open-source data, it provides a strong and reproducible baseline for the research community and industry. Teffic-Audio is trained only with open-source data and achieves the lowest EER on five individual test sets, showing a favorable performance-complexity trade-off compared with larger leading systems. The system does not rely on additional architectural complexity; instead, its training recipe is key to its generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 17, 04:00

**Background**: Speech deepfake detection aims to distinguish real human speech from synthetic or manipulated audio, which is increasingly important due to the rise of speech synthesis, voice conversion, and neural codec resynthesis. Conformer is a convolution-augmented transformer architecture that combines self-attention and convolution to capture both global and local features, making it effective for speech tasks. Multi-head attentive statistics pooling aggregates frame-level features into a sequence-level embedding, commonly used in speaker verification and anti-spoofing systems.
